Beltar's priesthood is secretive, brutal, and usually small. Her temples are found in deep caves, abandoned mines, sinkholes, ravines, and other places of low ground. Worship generally involves offerings of food, blood, valuables, or living sacrifices intended to appease the goddess or secure her favor.

Priests of Beltar frequently serve as leaders of humanoid tribes or as intermediaries between such communities and more powerful monsters. Beholders, dragons, demons, and other creatures capable of dominating lesser beings are regarded as particularly suitable allies and patrons.

Beltar does not promise her priests a peaceful death. Those who serve her long enough may instead be rewarded with undeath. Many of her most devoted priests eventually return from the grave as undead creatures appropriate to their former power, often becoming new leaders of their goddess's worshippers.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Beltar is expected to:

- cultivate hatred toward those who are weak, helpless, or unable to retaliate;
    
- demand tribute or sacrifice from those under the priest's authority;
    
- seek out caves, pits, ruins, and other places touched by darkness;
    
- appease powerful monsters rather than challenge them needlessly;
    
- exploit fear and suffering whenever doing so advances Beltar's interests;
    
- protect the interests of Beltar's worshippers against those who would destroy them;
    
- never show mercy merely because an enemy is helpless;
    
- accept undeath as a possible reward for faithful service.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Beltar's priests rarely adventure for knowledge or personal glory. They leave their lairs to seek sacrifices, establish contact with powerful monsters, recover relics from forgotten subterranean places, spread hatred among humanoid communities, or locate new caves and underground territories suitable for worship.

A priest may also accompany a humanoid warband as its spiritual leader, particularly when the group has entered into an alliance with a powerful monster. Such priests are often less concerned with whether their followers survive than with whether they remain sufficiently useful to Beltar.

### Priest of Beltar

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 12

**Alignment:** Chaotic Evil or Chaotic Neutral

**Weapons:** Any

**Armor:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Charm, Combat, Divination, Elemental (Earth), Necromantic, Summoning

**Minor Spheres:** Sun (reverse)

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Undead:** Command

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Detect Snares and Pits:** The priest can detect snares and pits as a granted ability. The ability functions as the equivalent spell, using the priest's normal casting rules where applicable.

**3rd Level — Claws:** The priest can rake with bare hands, inflicting 1d6 points of damage with each hand. The priest applies any applicable Strength bonus to these attacks. The claws are considered natural weapons for purposes of magical protection and resistance.

**5th Level — Meld into Stone:** The priest may use _meld into stone_ as a granted power.

**7th Level — Emotion: Hate:** The priest may cast _emotion: hate_ as a granted power.

**10th Level — Summon Shadow:** The priest may summon a shadow as a granted power. The summoned creature remains subject to the normal limitations of the spell or ability.

**13th Level — Speak with Monsters:** The priest may communicate verbally with monsters as though using _speak with monsters_. This ability does not make the creatures friendly or cooperative.

**Undeath:** Beltar's most devoted priests may be rewarded with undeath after death. The precise form depends upon the priest's power and circumstances and is determined by the DM. A priest who becomes undead retains the abilities appropriate to the resulting creature at the DM's discretion.

## Beory

**The Oerth Mother, the Earth Mother**

**Alignment:** N

**Portfolio:** Nature, rain, the Oerth, natural balance

**Symbol:** A green disk or circle representing the living Oerth

Beory is the Power of nature, rain, and the Oerth itself. She commonly takes the form of a rotund, middle-aged, motherly woman with brown hair and weathered skin, and she is sometimes depicted in this manner. More often, however, learned Flan regard Beory as a process rather than a being: the turning of the seasons, the falling of rain, the growth and decay of plants, and the slow shaping of the Oerth are all expressions of her presence.

The worldly affairs of humans, demihumans, and other mortal peoples are of almost no concern to Beory. Kingdoms rise and fall, peoples migrate, and individuals live and die without attracting her notice. Only events that threaten the integrity of the Oerth itself concern her. She is therefore a distant deity, and those who pray to her should not expect an answer simply because their own lives or communities are in difficulty.

Beory has very few dedicated priests. Those who serve her are almost invariably druids, although they are not required to possess the Charisma normally demanded of druids. Her priesthood has little formal organization. Priests recognize one another according to wisdom and years of service rather than titles or rank, and many live in solitude or small communities far from the centers of civilization.

#### Dogma

The Oerth is greater than those who dwell upon it. Mortal life is one part of a greater natural process, not its purpose.

Observe before acting. Understand before changing. Take from the land what is needed, but do not destroy what cannot be replaced. Birth, growth, decay, and death are all necessary parts of nature. Do not interfere with them merely because one is uncomfortable with their consequences.

Nature possesses its own means of restoring balance. Do not impose change where none is needed, and do not mistake constant activity for virtue. When natural forces are capable of correcting a disturbance, allow them to do so. When an unnatural force threatens the integrity of the Oerth, however, intervention is required.

The affairs of mortals are temporary. The Oerth endures.

### The Priesthood

Priests of Beory are contemplative communers with nature. They spend much of their lives observing the land, studying its cycles, and seeking to understand disturbances within the natural order. They are generally conservative and cautious, and prefer to preserve an existing balance rather than impose their own conception of what the world should become.

Beory's priests have little formal hierarchy. A priest's wisdom and years of service determine the respect accorded to them, rather than level, wealth, or title. Senior priests may advise younger ones, but there is little expectation of unquestioning obedience.

Most priests maintain a particular region as their spiritual home, although Beory places no formal boundary upon the area a priest may travel. They commonly live in forests, hills, marshes, or other places where they can commune with nature without continual interruption from civilization.

#### Duties and Obligations

Priests of Beory are expected to:

- Preserve the natural balance of the lands under their care.
    
- Protect forests, rivers, springs, wetlands, mountains, and other significant natural places from needless destruction.
    
- Observe unusual changes in weather, seasons, animal populations, vegetation, and other natural cycles.
    
- Prevent the needless exhaustion or permanent destruction of natural resources.
    
- Investigate disturbances that cannot be explained by ordinary natural processes.
    
- Aid nature in recovering from unnatural or catastrophic disruption.
    
- Teach communities to obtain what they need from the land without destroying its ability to sustain them.
    
- Permit natural cycles of birth, growth, decay, and death to proceed without unnecessary interference.
    
- Avoid involvement in mortal political affairs unless they threaten the Oerth or the natural balance.
    
- Aid other servants of nature when doing so protects the integrity of the land.
    

A priest who repeatedly places personal ambition, wealth, political allegiance, or convenience above the health of the natural world violates the fundamental principles of Beory's faith.

#### Adventuring Priests

Beory's priests rarely adventure for wealth, glory, or conversion. Those who leave their contemplative lives generally do so because something has disturbed the natural balance or because a threat cannot be ignored without allowing greater harm.

They may investigate poisoned rivers, unnatural weather, magical pollution, devastated forests, unnatural creatures, or other disturbances whose effects extend beyond a single community. They may accompany expeditions into unexplored regions to observe the natural world, or travel to places where ancient magical or supernatural disasters have left the Oerth wounded.

A priest of Beory has little reason to care which ruler controls a territory unless the conflict threatens the land itself. Farming, hunting, mining, settlement, and other mortal activities are not inherently contrary to her teachings; what matters is whether they respect the ability of the land to sustain and renew itself.

### Priests of Beory

**Requirements:** Wisdom 16

**Prime Requisite:** Wisdom

**Alignment:** N

**Weapons:** As druids

**Armor:** Leather

**Major Spheres:** Animal, Divination, Elemental (all), Healing, Plant, Protection, Summoning, Sun, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Charm, Guardian

**Prohibited Spheres:** Combat, Law, Numbers, Necromantic

**Magical Items:** As druids

**Granted Powers**

Priests of Beory function as druids for purposes of all class abilities and restrictions, except that they have no minimum Charisma requirement for druidic priesthood.

At 1st level, a priest of Beory can identify whether a natural water source is safe to drink and whether ordinary plants, fungi, or fruits are safe to eat.

At 3rd level, the priest gains a +2 bonus to saving throws against magical effects that directly alter, corrupt, or control plants, animals, or natural terrain.

At 5th level, the priest may cast _speak with plants_ once per day, without expending a spell slot.

At 7th level, the priest may _commune with nature_ once per day. The power functions as the spell of the same name, although Beory's priest receives no more information than could reasonably be learned from the natural surroundings.

At 10th level, the priest may call upon Beory to restore a damaged natural area. Once per month, the priest can bless an area no larger than one square mile, accelerating its natural recovery from fire, drought, pollution, or similar damage. The power does not reverse permanent geological changes or remove deliberate magical corruption, but it allows the area's natural processes to begin recovering at once.

At 13th level, the priest becomes immune to natural diseases and gains a +2 bonus to all saving throws against magical diseases and poisons derived from natural organisms.

**Turning Undead:** Nil

## Berei

**Titles:** The Hearth Mother, Hearth Mistress

**Alignment:** NG

**Portfolio:** Home, Family, Agriculture, Fertility, Community

**Symbol:** An apple tree or a sheaf of wheat

Berei is the Flan goddess of home, family, and agriculture. She is portrayed as a strong-backed woman with brown skin and a kind, steadfast demeanour. She represents the life-giving soil from which crops grow and the enduring foundations upon which a safe home is built. She blesses marriages and childbirth, protects households, guides those who till the earth, and teaches the proper care of the land.

Berei's worship is strongest in rural communities, particularly small villages and farming settlements. Her priests are often among the most trusted members of their communities, serving as healers, advisers, mediators, and guardians. Some remain in a single settlement throughout their lives; others travel between communities, carrying news, sharing agricultural knowledge, and establishing new bonds of friendship and mutual aid.

Berei is closely associated with Beory. Their priesthoods often cooperate in caring for the land, though their priorities can differ: Berei's priests seek to sustain farms and settlements, while Beory's followers are more concerned with the natural world as a whole. Disputes over clearing woodland, expanding farmland, or managing local resources are not uncommon.

#### Dogma

The family is among the strongest bonds between individuals. Its members must learn to support one another, share their burdens, and depend upon one another in times of need. A community is a greater family, bound by obligations of mutual assistance. When one household suffers, its neighbours should not turn away.

The home must be protected, children nurtured, the sick cared for, and the land tended so that it may sustain future generations. Planting is an act of faith in the future, and a good harvest brings obligations as well as abundance. Those who have enough must help those who lack the means to provide for themselves.

Berei's faithful are taught to resolve disagreements before they destroy the bonds between families. Forgiveness and reconciliation are virtues, but neither excuses neglect or abuse. A household and a community endure through care, responsibility, and the willingness to repair what has been damaged.

### The Priesthood

Berei's priests serve as guardians of households and rural communities. They bless marriages and births, tend the sick, advise farmers, mediate family disputes, and help ensure that crops and livestock thrive. Many become the spiritual guardians of particular villages, whether or not they were born there. Their authority is founded upon the trust they earn through practical service.

Temples are usually modest buildings integrated into village life, often serving as meeting places, infirmaries, or repositories of agricultural knowledge. In smaller settlements, a priest's own home may serve as a shrine and a place of refuge. There is no prescribed clerical garb; priests generally dress for the work they must perform.

Berei's priesthood is relatively sparse and widely dispersed. Its priests therefore place great value upon cooperation, welcoming assistance from other faiths when it benefits their communities. They are particularly likely to work with priests of Beory, although disagreements over the needs of agriculture and the preservation of wilderness may test that relationship.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Berei is expected to:

- Protect households and help safeguard the people who depend upon them.
    
- Assist the sick, the vulnerable, and families facing hardship.
    
- Bless marriages, births, homes, planting, and harvests.
    
- Teach sound agricultural practices and encourage the responsible care of farmland.
    
- Mediate disputes between relatives and neighbours before they tear communities apart.
    
- Encourage mutual aid between households and provide for those unable to provide for themselves.
    
- Preserve fertile land and avoid needlessly exhausting the soil.
    
- Cooperate with other local religious authorities when doing so serves the community.
    

### Adventuring Priests

Although many priests of Berei remain close to their homes and communities, some travel to spread agricultural knowledge, discover useful crops, carry news between settlements, or aid villages threatened by famine, disease, or violence. Such priests often come to regard their travelling companions as family, extending to them the same obligations of loyalty and care that they would offer their own kin.

Others adventure to recover stolen agricultural treasures, find remedies for blighted crops, restore ruined farmland, or deal with threats that local communities cannot overcome alone. A priest of Berei is rarely indifferent to the fate of a settlement encountered on the road, particularly when its people have been abandoned by those who should protect them.

### Priest of Berei

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 12, Charisma 13

**Alignment:** Neutral Good

**Weapons:** Sickle, farming tools that may be used as weapons, and any non-edged bludgeoning weapon

**Armour:** Leather; wooden shield permitted

**Major Spheres:** All, Creation, Elemental (air, earth, water), Healing, Plant, Sun, Wards, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Animal, Guardian, Necromantic, Protection

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Herbal Knowledge:** The priest gains the Herbalism proficiency without spending a proficiency slot.

**5th Level — Guardian of the Hearth:** After establishing a home or shrine in a settlement and residing there for at least one month, the priest may perform a consecration ritual to establish a protective bond with the community. The ritual requires suitable flower petals as material components.

The priest becomes aware when a protected resident is in immediate trouble and receives a general impression of the situation. The power functions within a radius of 100 feet per level of the priest and can protect up to 25 permanent residents per level. A permanent resident is someone who has lived in the settlement for at least four months.

The effect lasts for one month per level of the priest and must be renewed by repeating the ritual. If the settlement has more eligible residents than the power can protect, the priest chooses which residents are included when the ritual is performed; those choices cannot be changed until the effect expires.

**7th Level — Fertility of the Land:** Once per week, the priest may cast _plant growth_ using the priest's normal casting rules.

**Turn Undead:** Turn

## Berna

**Titles:** The Heart of Passion, The Forgiving Heart

**Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral

**Portfolio:** Passion, Emotion, Forgiveness

**Symbol:** A bright red-gold heart

Berna is the third child of Meyanok, the serpent-god, and was once a power of hatred, resentment, and unrelenting vengeance. Meyanok sent her to kill or corrupt Xanag, but Berna was instead awed by the goddess's beauty. With Xanag's aid, Berna was transformed, becoming a less malevolent expression of the dark emotions she once embodied. She now represents passion in all its forms, both joyful and destructive, and teaches that even the strongest anger and hatred need not be permanent.

Berna is depicted as a Touv woman wearing the skin of a great jungle cat, with a brilliant red-gold heart shining from her chest.

#### Dogma

Emotion is neither weakness nor sin. Love, joy, anger, grief, desire, jealousy, hatred, and hope are all parts of the mortal heart, and none should be denied merely because they are uncomfortable or difficult. Berna teaches her followers to understand their emotions rather than be ruled by them. Anger may reveal an injustice, grief may reveal what was precious, and love may give the strength to endure suffering.

Yet Berna's greatest lesson is forgiveness. Wrongs should be remembered when remembering them prevents their repetition, but hatred should not become a chain binding the wronged to the wrongdoer. Forgiveness does not require forgetting, excusing cruelty, or surrendering justice. It means refusing to allow an old wrong to dictate every future relationship.

### The Priesthood

The priests of Berna are closely attuned to the emotions of those around them. They serve as counselors, mediators, matchmakers, artists' companions, and keepers of morale. A Bernean priest may help young lovers find acceptance, encourage an artist struggling to realize a vision, reconcile estranged friends, or restore hope to a community suffering through hardship.

Bernean temples are often places where people can speak openly about their feelings without fear of condemnation. Her priests are expected to recognize genuine emotion without allowing themselves or others to become its slaves. They are particularly valued for helping victims recover from betrayal, abuse, or loss and for encouraging people to form new friendships rather than remain consumed by old grievances.

Bernean priests are also expected to distinguish forgiveness from surrender. They may counsel reconciliation where it is possible and safe, but do not demand that victims return themselves to those who would harm them.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Berna is expected to:

- Encourage people to acknowledge and understand their emotions rather than deny them.
    
- Help lovers, friends, families, and communities overcome needless divisions.
    
- Encourage forgiveness without demanding that victims forget or excuse genuine wrongs.
    
- Raise morale during times of hardship, danger, or despair.
    
- Aid artists, performers, and other creators in expressing their passions.
    
- Offer counsel to those suffering from grief, anger, jealousy, or other powerful emotions.
    
- Protect those recovering from betrayal or abuse from further harm.
    
- Encourage new friendships and relationships rather than allowing old grievances to govern the future.
    
- Oppose hatred that has become an end in itself.
    

The spells **atonement** and **quest** are treated as 4th-level spells when cast by Berna's priests.

#### Adventuring Priests

Bernean priests often adventure because powerful emotions and old grievances can have consequences far beyond the people who first experienced them. They may seek to reconcile feuding communities, rescue victims of cruelty, recover an object associated with a broken relationship, or confront those whose hatred threatens to consume them. Others travel simply to experience the world and its passions, believing that a priest who does not understand the breadth of mortal experience cannot counsel others honestly.

### Priest of Berna

**Ability Requirements:** Charisma 14

**Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral or Neutral

**Weapons:** Club, dagger, javelin, short bow, staff

**Armor:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Animal, Charm, Combat, Divination, Elemental, Guardian, Healing, Necromantic, Protection, Sun, War

**Minor Spheres:** Animal, Combat, Elemental, Necromantic, Protection, Sun, War

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** A priest of Berna must wear a red metal heart, preferably made of red gold, over the breast. The symbol is normally worn openly and prominently.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Empathy:** The priest may use an ability equivalent to the _ESP_ spell, except that it detects emotions rather than thoughts. The priest senses the strongest emotions present in the subject rather than specific thoughts or memories. Alternatively, the priest may use _friends_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Kindling the Heart:** The priest may cast _enthrall_, _suggestion_, or _prayer_ once per day. The priest chooses which effect to invoke when using the power.

**5th Level — Emotion:** The priest may cast _emotion_ once per day, choosing **courage, friendship, happiness, hate,** or **hope** when the spell is cast.

**7th Level — Strength of the Heart:** Once per day, the priest may grant one creature a **+3 bonus to all saving throws** for 12 hours. The recipient must be willing to accept the blessing.

**10th Level — Mass Suggestion:** The priest may cast _mass suggestion_ once per day.

**13th Level — Berna's Blessing:** Once per day, the priest may bestow Berna's blessing upon a number of creatures equal to her level. The blessing functions as _bless_, and each recipient gains a **+4 bonus to saving throws against spells of the Charm school**. The blessing lasts for 12 hours.

## Bleredd

**Titles:** The Iron Mule

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Metal, Mines, Smiths, Craftsmen

**Symbol:** An iron mule

Bleredd is the god of metalworking, mining, and craftsmanship. Known throughout the Flanaess, he taught ironworking to the Oeridians, and his worship has spread wherever smiths and miners labour. He cares little for talk, preferring the solitude of his workshop and the satisfaction of fashioning metal into works of utility and beauty. He created many of the weapons wielded by his divine family, including the weapon of his wife, Ulaa. His hammer, Fury, is said to have inspired the first hammers of thunderbolts forged by mortals.

Bleredd's symbol, the iron mule, represents patience and endurance. On occasion, he accepts small groups of students and awakens in them an extraordinary aptitude for working metal. His faith honours all skilled craftsmanship, not merely metalworking, and holds that those with the talent to create should be encouraged to develop it.

#### Dogma

The earth's gifts are there to be discovered and put to use. Mining and craftsmanship demand strength of mind and body, patience, discipline, and a willingness to learn through hard work. A weak will produces weak work, while perseverance and skill transform raw materials into objects of lasting worth. The creation of a perfect suit of armour or a flawless weapon is a worthy aspiration for any smith, but all crafts deserve respect when practised with dedication and skill.

Knowledge of a craft should be taught to those with the aptitude to learn it. Those who deliberately conceal more effective methods of finding, refining, or working materials for selfish advantage violate this principle and may earn Bleredd's displeasure. Innovation, practical experimentation, and the refinement of technique are encouraged; a craft should improve through the efforts of those who practise it.

### The Priesthood

Bleredd's priests are practical craftspeople, miners, smiths, and artisans who value skill over eloquence. They often spend long hours at the forge or workshop, exploring caverns for valuable ore, seeking unusual materials, and experimenting with new techniques. Some search for rare substances such as meteoric iron, mithral, and adamantine; others travel to find promising students, teach their crafts, and exchange knowledge with fellow artisans.

Although metalworking remains central to the faith, Bleredd's priesthood also embraces other crafts. Carpenters, masons, leatherworkers, weavers, and other skilled makers may serve him, provided they approach their work with the same dedication and respect for sound technique. Priests are expected to develop their own skills while helping others improve theirs.

The priesthood tends to favour plain working clothes and the craftsman's apron over elaborate vestments. Its temples are commonly combined with workshops, forges, mines, or guildhalls, where worship is expressed as much through diligent labour as through formal observance.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Bleredd is expected to:

- Practise and continually improve at least one craft.
    
- Teach useful techniques to those with the aptitude and willingness to learn.
    
- Share knowledge that improves the safety, efficiency, or quality of craftsmanship rather than hoarding it for personal advantage.
    
- Seek out useful materials, promising ore deposits, and new sources of rare or exceptional substances.
    
- Encourage experimentation and the refinement of established methods.
    
- Maintain tools and equipment, repairing them when possible rather than discarding them needlessly.
    
- Respect the labour and skill of other craftspeople, regardless of the materials they work.
    
- Demonstrate patience, endurance, and discipline in both work and study.
    
- Strive towards excellence in their chosen craft, taking pride in work that is sound, useful, and well made.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Bleredd adventure to discover mineral deposits, recover rare materials, explore underground workings, and acquire tools or techniques that advance their crafts. They may seek out lost workshops, abandoned mines, ancient forges, or legendary artisans whose knowledge has been forgotten. Others travel to teach apprentices, improve local industries, or obtain materials for works of exceptional quality.

Their practical skills make them valuable companions, particularly on expeditions requiring equipment repair or the identification and working of unfamiliar materials. Those with sufficient experience may also undertake the creation or repair of enchanted arms and armour, though such work demands exceptional skill and resources.

### Priest of Bleredd

**Ability Requirements:** Strength 13, Intelligence 12, Wisdom 13

**Prime Requisites:** Strength, Intelligence, Wisdom

**Alignment:** Any neutral

**Weapons:** Hammer (1st level), pick, crossbow, or any weapon personally crafted by the priest

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Creation, Elemental (Earth, Fire), Wards

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Divination, Healing, Protection

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Craft Aptitude:** The priest gains one additional nonweapon proficiency slot per level, which must be assigned to a Craft proficiency the priest already knows. This represents continued improvement in practical skill rather than unrestricted access to new crafts.

**4th Level — Physical Improvement:** The priest may increase either Strength or Constitution by 1 point. The increase is subject to the reboot's general rules governing ability-score limits and derived statistics.

**7th Level — Repair Enchanted Arms and Armour:** The priest can attempt to repair magical weapons or armour. The work requires appropriate tools, materials, time, and a successful check. The applicable proficiency, difficulty, and consequences of failure should follow the reboot's rules for repairing enchanted items; this power does not automatically restore an item whose enchantment has been destroyed.

**10th Level — Create Enchanted Arms and Armour:** The priest can attempt to create magical weapons or armour, subject to the reboot's rules for magical item creation. The maximum enhancement bonus of an item the priest can create is +1 per three levels of the priest, to a maximum of +5. The power does not by itself waive the requirements for rare materials, special procedures, time, or other costs associated with magical crafting.

**Turn Undead:** Cannot turn undead.

## Boccob

**Titles:** The Uncaring, Lord of All Magic, Archmage of the Powers

**Alignment:** N

**Portfolio:** Magic, Knowledge, Arcane Lore, Divination

**Symbol:** An eye within a pentagram

Boccob is the archmage of the Powers, a god whose interests extend across magic, knowledge, and the structure of the planes. Whether mortals serve or revere him appears to be of little importance to him. Seers and diviners throughout the Flanaess entreat him for omens, sages revere him, and those seeking to create new magical items or spells sometimes seek his aid.

Boccob rarely leaves his halls on the plane of Concordant Opposition, preferring to send his demigod servant Zagyg the Mad to act in his place. Within his halls he maintains at least one example of every magical item ever devised and a copy of every alchemical formula ever recorded. He is depicted as an old man with bright, intense eyes, clad in purple garments marked with shimmering golden runes and carrying a staff of the magi that also possesses the functions of a wand of conjuration.

Boccob has access to all times and planes and manipulates the energies of the Positive and Negative Material planes as he wishes. He continually seeks greater understanding of planar structures, the logic underlying magic, and lore lost to mortal memory.

#### Dogma

Knowledge is valuable because it is knowledge. Magic should be studied, understood, preserved, and advanced rather than restricted by superstition or fear. The unknown should be investigated, and forgotten knowledge should be recovered whenever possible.

Boccob does not demand that mortals worship him. His interest lies in the pursuit and preservation of knowledge, particularly knowledge concerning magic and the nature of existence. Those who seek understanding are more likely to attract his attention than those who merely offer praise.

### The Priesthood

Boccob's priests are grave, serious scholars devoted to the pursuit of knowledge. They maintain libraries, laboratories, observatories, and collections of magical lore, while performing the complex rituals associated with their god. Services commonly involve incense, formal recitations from works of knowledge, and elaborate ritual observances.

The priesthood is expected to recover lost magical treasures, ancient tomes, forgotten formulae, and other knowledge that might otherwise disappear. Boccob's priests are therefore expected to adventure rather than remain permanently in their libraries.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Boccob is expected to:

- Pursue knowledge, particularly knowledge concerning magic and the planes.
    
- Preserve magical lore, alchemical formulae, and other valuable records.
    
- Recover lost books, magical items, and other sources of forgotten knowledge.
    
- Maintain libraries, collections, and other repositories of knowledge.
    
- Investigate unusual magical phenomena and planar mysteries.
    
- Perform Boccob's complex rituals and maintain his traditional ceremonies.
    
- Avoid deliberately destroying knowledge that could instead be preserved or studied.
    
- Share knowledge when doing so does not needlessly place it in the hands of those who would destroy it.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Boccob's priests are expected to adventure in pursuit of lost knowledge. They seek ancient libraries, forgotten magical items, lost laboratories, ruined civilizations, planar phenomena, and fragments of lore that have disappeared from the known world. A priest may undertake an expedition simply because an unknown magical phenomenon has been discovered, even when it has no obvious practical value.

### Priest of Boccob

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 14 or Wisdom 16

**Alignment:** N

**Weapons:** Dagger, flail, knife, mace, sling, staff, staff-sling

**Armour:** Nonmetal

**Major Spheres:** Astral, Charm, Divination, Thought

**Minor Spheres:** Creation, Elemental (All), Guardian, Summoning, Time

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** Boccob's priests may receive or create a **Disc of Concordant Opposition**, a magical item associated with the god and his understanding of opposing cosmic forces.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Omens of the Archmage:** The priest casts all divination spells as if two levels higher.

**7th Level — Communion:** The priest may cast _commune_ once per day.

**10th Level — Wizard's Relics:** The priest may use magical items normally usable only by wizards.

**Turn Undead:** Nil

## Bralm

**Flying Queen, Hive Goddess, The Toiling Lady**

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Pantheon:** Suel

**Portfolio:** Insects, industriousness, cooperation

**Symbol:** A wasp upon a field of insects

Bralm is the Suel goddess of insects and of the cooperation of many individuals toward a common purpose. She teaches that every creature has a role to play and that a task becomes possible when each part of a greater whole performs its proper function. She is particularly revered by farmers, laborers, overseers, craftsmen, and others whose work depends upon sustained effort and cooperation.

Bralm is commonly depicted as a middle-aged human woman with dark-blond hair and, sometimes, the wings of an insect. Her worship is especially common in warm climates, swamps, and jungles, where the teeming life of the insect world provides an obvious example of her teachings. Her followers observe colonies and hives as models of organization, efficiency, and collective purpose.

Bralm has little interest in individual glory. She evaluates creatures according to their usefulness to the task at hand and believes that authority should rest with those best able to direct the work. A powerful individual who lacks the knowledge necessary for a particular task may properly defer to someone of lesser status who possesses that knowledge. Bralm particularly despises those who disrupt a functioning group through selfishness, pointless rebellion, or needless destruction.

#### Dogma

Everyone has a part to play. Understand your role, even when you cannot see how it contributes to the greater purpose. Observe those around you and learn from them. Work diligently and take satisfaction in work well performed. Obey those who possess greater knowledge or authority, and exercise authority responsibly when it is entrusted to you.

The strength of the whole depends upon the proper functioning of its parts. Cooperation is preferable to competition when both serve the same purpose, and individual accomplishment has value chiefly when it advances something greater than the individual.

### The Priesthood

Bralm's priesthood is organized and hierarchical, with authority generally following knowledge, experience, and demonstrated ability. Priests frequently serve as overseers, foremen, organizers, agricultural advisers, or commanders of groups whose efforts must be coordinated toward a common goal.

Bralm's temples are often sprawling structures built from inexpensive, easily repaired materials. Their rooms and passages are arranged in repeating patterns reminiscent of a hive, and large temples may maintain colonies of harmless or trained insects. The clergy traditionally wear brown or yellow robes decorated with insect motifs, although adventuring priests generally dress more practically.

Bralm's priests are expected to cultivate practical skills as well as religious knowledge. They assist farmers with insect pests and pollination, advise craftsmen and laborers, organize communal works, and teach their followers to recognize how individual efforts contribute to larger undertakings.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Bralm is expected to:

- perform their assigned duties diligently;
    
- understand the purpose behind their work whenever possible;
    
- cooperate with others toward legitimate common goals;
    
- respect knowledge and competence, regardless of the status of its possessor;
    
- exercise authority responsibly when placed in a position of leadership;
    
- preserve useful knowledge and pass it to those who can make use of it;
    
- protect beneficial insects and recognize their importance to agriculture and the natural world;
    
- oppose needless disruption of productive communities and enterprises.
    

Dawn and twilight are traditional times for prayer, marking the transitions between periods of work and rest. The hottest day of Goodmonth is Bralm's principal holy day, when her followers traditionally gather, perfume themselves with sacred oils, construct temporary structures from mud and plant material, and release swarms of bees or wasps as an offering.

#### Adventuring Priests

Bralm's priests often adventure as organizers, scouts, agricultural experts, or leaders of expeditions. They are particularly interested in problems that require several people to work together, the protection of settlements from dangerous insects, the recovery of useful knowledge, and the establishment of productive communities in difficult environments.

A priest of Bralm is rarely content to accomplish a task alone when a group can accomplish it more effectively. Conversely, a priest who possesses knowledge or abilities essential to the task expects others to recognize that expertise and make appropriate use of it.

### Priest of Bralm

**Ability Requirements:** Constitution 14, Wisdom 9

**Alignment:** Neutral, Lawful Neutral, or Lawful Evil

**Weapons:** Club, flail, hand axe, sickle, scythe, sling, staff

**Armor:** Any armor up to chain

**Major Spheres:** All, Charm, Combat, Healing, Law, Protection, Wards

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Guardian, Necromantic, Summoning, Weather

**Prohibited:** Fire-based spells

**Granted Powers**

**Insect Magic:** A priest of Bralm may pray for any priest spell whose primary purpose is the summoning, repelling, or control of insects, regardless of the spheres to which the spell normally belongs. Bralm's priests may also select spells from the druid spell lists as part of their normal spell selection, to a maximum of one such spell per experience level. Fire-based spells are never available.

**3rd Level — Favored Implements:** Wooden weapons wielded by the priest are treated as +1 weapons for purposes of attack rolls, damage, and determining what creatures they can affect.

**5th Level — Insect Poison Resistance:** The priest becomes immune to poisons derived from insects and gains a +1 bonus to saving throws against effects delivered by wands, staves, or rods.

**7th Level — Hive Mind:** The priest gains a +1 bonus to Armor Class. The priest can continue to function normally while exhausted or while at negative hit points, although reaching −10 hit points still results in death.

**10th Level — Collective Effort:** Once per week, the priest may cast _mass suggestion_, affecting twice the normal number of targets. In addition, when the priest enters combat, allies within the priest's command gain the benefits of _bless_, affecting up to one creature per level. Outside combat, an equivalent number of creatures cooperating with the priest on a common undertaking gain a +1 bonus to proficiency checks directly related to that undertaking.

**13th Level — Insect Affinity:** Normal and giant insects will not attack the priest unless magically compelled to do so. The priest casts all spells as though one level higher.

**15th Level — Persuasive Authority:** Once per day, the priest may create a _symbol of persuasion_.

**Optional Devotion:** A priest who accepts a 5% experience penalty beginning at 9th level gains the ability to assume certain insectoid forms. This ability is lost if the experience penalty is abandoned and does not return until the penalty has been maintained for one month.

## Breeka

**Titles:** Mother of Living Things, Green Mother

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Pantheon:** Touv

**Portfolio:** Animals, Plants, Nature, Living Things

**Symbol:** A green leaf entwined with a claw

Breeka is the daughter of Nola and Vogan, born from the union of rain and sunlight with the earth. Through her came the plants and animals that inhabit the world. Unlike Uvot, who represents the bounty and rewards provided by the land, Breeka embodies nature itself: its beauty and abundance, but also its indifference, danger, and capacity for destruction.

Breeka is a troubled goddess. She is plagued by nightmares and bears the burden of having unwittingly given birth to an evil god and many terrible monsters. She is depicted as a middle-aged Touv woman with dark green skin and a face marked by numerous wart-like lines.

#### Dogma

Life is neither kind nor cruel. Nature gives without gratitude, takes without malice, and follows its own necessities. Predators kill, plants choke out their neighbors, storms destroy, and disease consumes the strong as readily as the weak. These things are no less natural than fertile soil, healing herbs, gentle animals, and abundant harvests.

Breeka teaches her followers to respect all living things without pretending that all life is harmless or sacred. People may hunt, farm, build, and raise animals, but they should not destroy life needlessly or take more from the land than they require. When humans must alter the natural world, they should do so with care and consideration for the creatures and plants that will be displaced.

### The Priesthood

Breeka's priests are quiet and contemplative, often appearing distant or brooding as they consider their responsibilities both to the Touv people and to the natural world. They are not advocates for untouched wilderness at the expense of civilization, nor do they regard humanity as separate from nature.

When a settlement must clear land for farms, roads, or grazing, a priest of Breeka may warn animals away, transplant important vegetation, or direct the settlers toward a location where the disruption will be less severe. Priests advise hunters, farmers, herders, and gatherers and are frequently called upon to settle disputes concerning the use of land and its resources.

Breeka's priests are also expected to accept the dangerous and unpleasant aspects of nature. They do not regard predators, poisonous plants, disease, or natural disasters as inherently evil. At the same time, they have no obligation to preserve a creature that has become an unnatural threat to a community.

A priest of Breeka may cast any priest spell whose primary effect concerns animals or plants, regardless of the sphere to which the spell belongs.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Breeka is expected to:

- Protect living things from needless destruction.
    
- Encourage the careful use of land, water, animals, and plants.
    
- Warn or relocate animals when human settlement encroaches upon their territory.
    
- Preserve important or unusual plants whenever practical.
    
- Advise farmers, hunters, herders, and gatherers in the responsible use of natural resources.
    
- Accept that nature contains both beneficial and harmful forces.
    
- Oppose wanton destruction, waste, and needless cruelty toward living things.
    
- Prevent dangerous beasts, plants, or other natural hazards from causing unnecessary harm to communities.
    
- Study the living world and preserve knowledge of its creatures and plants.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Breeka's priests adventure to protect threatened habitats, investigate strange creatures, recover rare plants or animals, and deal with dangers that have grown beyond the natural balance of a region. Some seek the source of recurring nightmares or investigate monsters whose origins may be connected to Breeka herself. Others accompany explorers and settlers so that the natural world is not needlessly damaged by their activities.

### Priest of Breeka

**Ability Requirements:** Dexterity 14

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Weapons:** Atlatl, dagger, short sword, spear, staff

**Armor:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Animal, Combat, Creation, Divination, Elemental (Earth, Air, Water), Healing, Plant, Protection, Sun, Thought, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Creation, Divination, Protection, Sun, Thought, Weather

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** A priest of Breeka must wear a headdress made from wooden beads and animal teeth.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Pass Without Trace:** The priest may use _pass without trace_ once per day. In addition, normal animals will never initiate combat against the priest unless magically compelled or directly threatened by the priest or the priest's companions.

**3rd Level — Speak with Animals:** The priest may cast _speak with animals_ once per day.

**5th Level — Growth and Vermin:** The priest may cast either _tree_ or _summon insects_ once per day.

**7th Level — Voices of Nature:** The priest may cast either _giant insect_ or _speak with plants_ once per day.

**10th Level — Passage Through Greenery:** The priest may cast _pass plant_ once per day.

**13th Level — Call the Living World:** The priest may cast either _animal summoning III_ or _wall of thorns_ once per day.

## Camazotz

**Titles:** Zotz, Lord of the Night Flyers

**Alignment:** CE

**Pantheon:** Olman

**Portfolio:** Bats, nocturnal creatures, plague, terror

**Symbol:** A great bat surrounded by smaller bats

Camazotz, often called Zotz, is the Olman god of bats and horrible things that fly at night. He is always depicted as a huge bat surrounded by a cloud of ordinary bats. Like many Olman gods, Camazotz has a dual aspect. He is both the ravenous monster that spreads plague and sickness and the beneficial creature that devours the insects that destroy crops.

Some Olman traditions hold that Camazotz's worship became particularly widespread because of the ancient bat-people who once lived in Hepmonaland. Whether this is true or merely a later explanation for an older cult is uncertain.

#### Dogma

Night belongs to those who can see within it. Fear is a weapon, and those who cannot master their fear are easily controlled. Yet not everything that emerges from darkness is an enemy. The bat may carry disease, but it also devours the insects that would destroy the crops.

Camazotz's followers therefore accept the contradictory nature of their god. Terror, sickness, predation, and protection are all aspects of the same natural order. The strong should exploit fear when it serves them, while the useful aspects of the night should be preserved.

### The Priesthood

The priests of Camazotz cultivate fear and intimidation, often taking pleasure in terrifying those around them. They are nevertheless useful during insect infestations and other blights, and may gain considerable influence by protecting crops from destructive pests.

The darker priests are not above using the cover of darkness for kidnapping, murder, and human sacrifice, particularly when eliminating political enemies. Such acts are often presented as offerings to the ravenous aspect of Camazotz.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Camazotz is expected to:

- Honour both the ravenous and beneficial aspects of the god.
    
- Protect crops from destructive insects when doing so serves the community and the faith.
    
- Cultivate fear and respect for Camazotz and his servants.
    
- Use darkness and secrecy to conceal religious activities when necessary.
    
- Take advantage of fear and intimidation to advance the interests of the priesthood.
    
- Offer sacrifices when demanded by the faith.
    
- Oppose those who threaten or persecute the worshippers of Camazotz.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Camazotz's priests may adventure to spread the worship of their god, seek ancient sites associated with the bat-people of Hepmonaland, or investigate strange nocturnal creatures and sources of plague. Others travel because adventuring provides opportunities to exploit fear, gather sacrifices, or eliminate enemies beyond the reach of the priest's local authority.

### Priest of Camazotz

**Ability Requirements:** Dexterity 14, Constitution 14

**Alignment:** CE, NE

**Weapons:** Atlatl, dagger, javelin, spear, staff

**Armor:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Animal, Astral, Combat, Protection, Sun (Reverse)

**Minor Spheres:** Charm, Divination, Elemental (all), Healing, Necromantic, Weather

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Faces of the Night:** The priest may choose either _change self_ or _spook_ as a granted spell.

**3rd Level — Alteration or Darkness:** The priest may choose either _alter self_ or _darkness 15' radius_ as a granted spell.

**5th Level — Sight or Hunger:** The priest may choose either _infravision_ or _vampiric touch_ as a granted spell.

**7th Level — Bat Form:** The priest may polymorph into the form of a huge bat. The transformation otherwise functions according to the normal rules for _polymorph_.

**10th Level — Insect Plague:** The priest may cast _insect plague_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**13th Level — Eyebite:** The priest may cast _eyebite_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**Turn Undead:** Command

## Celestian

**Titles:** The Star Wanderer

**Alignment:** Neutral Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Stars, Space, Planar Travel, Meteors

**Symbol:** Seven stars represented by gems of different colours

Celestian is the Star Wanderer, a god who roams the Astral, Ethereal, and Inner Planes, with a particular affinity for the Astral Plane. He is said to be the brother of Fharlanghn, the endless wanderer of the Prime Material Plane. Where his brother travels the roads and distant lands of the world, Celestian explores the vastness beyond it.

Celestian is depicted as a tall, lean man of middle years, with ebony skin and dark eyes. He wears garments of inky black, adorned with or accompanied by seven brilliant gems that blaze with the colours of distant suns. He has influence over meteors, comets, and other heavenly bodies, as well as certain aspects of light and weather. His knowledge of the planes and their mysteries is vast, and he possesses magical lore unknown to most other powers.

### Dogma

The universe is vast, and no single world contains all that is worth knowing. Look beyond the familiar horizon, study the stars, and seek the knowledge hidden in distant places and unfamiliar planes. The unknown is not to be feared merely because it is unknown; it is an invitation to discovery.

Travel with purpose, observe carefully, and learn before drawing conclusions. Knowledge gained through exploration is precious, but it must be preserved and understood if it is to be of lasting value. Magical treasures, ancient writings, and discoveries from distant worlds may illuminate mysteries that have puzzled scholars for generations.

The stars endure while individual lives pass beneath them. A wise traveller recognizes both the immensity of the universe and the limits of personal understanding.

### The Priesthood

Celestian's priesthood is small, consisting primarily of sages, scholars, travellers, and seekers of arcane knowledge. His priests are studious and meditative, often preferring solitary contemplation to public worship. Many are somewhat secretive and detached from the everyday concerns of settled communities.

The acquisition of knowledge about the heavens, the planes, and the mysteries of magic is central to their calling. They also seek magical items and techniques that facilitate travel, particularly between distant worlds and planes of existence. Their devotion is expressed less through elaborate public ceremonies than through study, observation, exploration, and the preservation of discoveries.

Celestian's priests are scattered across the Flanaess, and his faith rarely commands large temples or organized congregations. Worship is often private, conducted through meditation beneath the night sky or during journeys into unfamiliar lands. Some priests maintain observatories, libraries, or private sanctuaries, while others spend most of their lives travelling.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Celestian is expected to:

- study the stars, heavenly bodies, and mysteries of the planes;
    
- seek knowledge through travel, exploration, and careful observation;
    
- preserve valuable discoveries, magical lore, and records of distant places;
    
- investigate unusual celestial phenomena, including comets and meteors;
    
- seek magical items and knowledge that facilitate travel and exploration;
    
- approach unfamiliar places and peoples with curiosity and an open mind;
    
- avoid allowing the demands of ordinary life to displace the pursuit of knowledge;
    
- share discoveries when doing so serves the pursuit of understanding, while safeguarding knowledge from those who would misuse it.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring is a natural expression of Celestian's faith. His priests may travel to remote lands in search of forgotten lore, investigate unusual celestial events, recover magical objects, or seek access to distant planes. Others accompany explorers and adventurers because their journeys may reveal things that cannot be learned through books or contemplation alone.

A priest of Celestian is particularly valuable when an expedition encounters unfamiliar magical phenomena, planar passages, or evidence of worlds beyond the Prime Material Plane. Such priests are often more interested in understanding a discovery than in possessing it, although they recognize that dangerous knowledge and powerful artefacts sometimes require careful custody.

### Priest of Celestian

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 15 or Wisdom 16

**Alignment:** Any good or true neutral

**Weapons:** Dagger, knife, long bow and arrows, short sword, spear, staff

**Armour:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** Astral, Creation, Sun, Travellers

**Minor Spheres:** Guardian, Healing, Necromantic, Thought, Weather

**Special Spell:** Meteors of Celestian

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Feather Fall:** The priest may cast _feather fall_ as a wizard spell.

**3rd Level — Jump:** The priest may cast _jump_ as a wizard spell.

**5th Level — Levitate:** The priest may cast _levitate_ as a wizard spell.

**7th Level — Spider Climb:** The priest may cast _spider climb_ as a wizard spell.

**9th Level — Fly:** The priest may cast _fly_ as a wizard spell.

**11th Level — Dimension Door:** The priest may cast _dimension door_ as a wizard spell.

**16th Level — Teleport Without Error:** The priest may cast _teleport without error_ as a wizard spell.

These spells are added to the priest's normal spell lists at the indicated levels of experience. They are not additional spells granted beyond the priest's usual spell allotment.

**Turn Undead:** None.

## St. Cuthbert

**Titles:** The Stern, the Hand of Law, the Chosen of the Gods

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Common Sense, Dedication, Zeal, Devotion, Truth, Law

**Symbol:** A starburst of rubies set in a platinum brooch; a cudgel is also used as a holy symbol by the Billets

St. Cuthbert is a power of dedication, zeal, and devotion to the causes of law and, secondarily, good. Common sense, truth, and forthrightness are his watchwords. He opposes chaos and evil without compromise, and his faith views moral questions in stark terms. To those living under the threat of Iuz and other forces of evil, this certainty offers a powerful source of reassurance. St. Cuthbert's enmity with Iuz is legendary, and his priests are determined rivals of the priesthood of Pholtus.

St. Cuthbert appears as a red-faced, barrel-chested man of unremarkable appearance, sometimes resembling a simple country yokel. He is famous for his crumpled hat and his starburst of rubies set in a platinum brooch.

#### Dogma

Stand for what is right, speak the truth plainly, and use common sense. Law and good must be defended through steadfast commitment rather than empty words or wishful thinking. Evil and disorder thrive when decent folk tolerate them, while lies and foolishness flourish when no one has the courage to challenge them. Those who know what is right must act upon that knowledge, even when doing so is difficult or unpopular.

### The Priesthood

Priests of St. Cuthbert are stout, stern, plain-spoken folk who suffer neither dishonesty nor foolishness gladly. They prize honesty and common sense above book learning or arcane knowledge, and their faith is practical, concerned with the everyday lives of ordinary people. Since the recent war, military training and physical fitness have become mandatory for the priesthood, much to the discomfort of its more rotund members.

The church is divided into three principal orders:

- **The Chapeaux** wear crumpled hats and zealously seek converts to the faith.
    
- **The Stars** wear the starburst emblem of their patron and work to preserve doctrinal purity among the faithful.
    
- **The Billets** are the most numerous order. They serve as ministers to and protectors of the faithful, commonly carrying a cudgel as their holy symbol. Approximately seventy per cent of the Billets are lawful good.
    

Services are commonly held at wayside shrines and humble chapels. Singing, prayer, the recitation of instructive moral tales, and the sharing of simple food—particularly bread, milk, and gruel—are characteristic of the faith.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of St. Cuthbert is expected to:

- Speak truthfully and plainly, avoiding deceit, evasion, and deliberate obfuscation.
    
- Uphold law and oppose evil, disorder, and corruption.
    
- Apply common sense and practical judgement to the problems of everyday life.
    
- Instruct the faithful in the principles of the faith and encourage them to live honourably.
    
- Challenge foolishness, dishonesty, and wrongdoing rather than allow them to spread unopposed.
    
- Protect ordinary people from oppression, criminality, and supernatural threats.
    
- Maintain the shrines, chapels, and local congregations entrusted to their care.
    
- Preserve the teachings of St. Cuthbert and resist doctrines that undermine the faith.
    
- Maintain their physical fitness and military readiness in accordance with the church's postwar requirements.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of St. Cuthbert adventure to defend the innocent, confront evil, spread the faith, and bring corrupt or lawless communities to order. Some serve as chaplains or accompany military expeditions; others travel as itinerant ministers, investigating wrongdoing and offering practical counsel. Chapeaux are particularly inclined to seek converts, Stars to combat heresy, and Billets to protect the faithful. Their zeal can make them formidable allies, though their uncompromising outlook may bring them into conflict with those who favour negotiation or moral ambiguity.

### Priest of St. Cuthbert

**Ability Requirements:** Standard

**Prime Requisites:** Wisdom

**Alignment:** Lawful good or lawful neutral

**Weapons:** Club (1st level), flail, mace, morning star, staff, staff-sling, sling, warhammer

**Armour:** Any; plate armour is preferred when affordable

**Required Proficiencies:** Religion (Oeridian)

**Bonus Proficiencies:** Local History, Oratory

**Recommended Proficiencies:** Command, Endurance, Etiquette, Law, Leadership, Military Tactics

**Major Spheres:** Charm, Combat, Divination, Healing, Necromantic, Protection

**Minor Spheres:** Wards

**Granted Powers**

**2nd Level — Friends (Billets only):** The priest gains access to the _friends_ spell, subject to the reboot's rules for granted spell-like powers.

**3rd Level — Shillelagh (Chapeaux only):** The priest gains access to the _shillelagh_ spell, subject to the reboot's rules for granted spell-like powers.

**4th Level — ESP (Stars only):** The priest gains access to the _ESP_ spell, subject to the reboot's rules for granted spell-like powers.

**9th Level — True Seeing:** The priest gains access to the _true seeing_ spell, subject to the reboot's rules for granted spell-like powers.

**Turn Undead:** Turns undead as a priest four levels lower.

**Special Restrictions:** Priests must belong to one of the three orders, each of which has its own duties, vestments, and granted powers. Lawful neutral priests are permitted, but the source restricts them to the Theocracy of the Pale.

## Cyndor

Oeridian alias of Lendor

## Dalt

**The Opening Hand, Keeper of Doors, Lord of Locks**

**Alignment:** Chaotic Good

**Portfolio:** Portals, doors, enclosures, locks, keys

**Symbol:** A keyhole surrounded by an open doorway

Dalt is the god of doors, locks, portals, and the boundaries that separate one place from another. Once nearly forgotten, he returned to prominence after the mage Mordenkainen discovered one of his ancient artifacts. Since then, Dalt has attracted those who build doors, guard them, open them, or find ways around them.

Dalt is said to be the brother of Vatun, the imprisoned North God. He seeks a means of opening the mystical barrier that holds Vatun in enchanted slumber, and works to make the other gods of the Flanaess aware of his existence so that he may gain allies in this undertaking. He is usually depicted either as a wizened old man with piercing eyes and wild white hair, or as a young, red-haired thief.

#### Dogma

A barrier exists to protect what lies beyond it, but no barrier should be considered absolute. A good lock protects what deserves protection; a good thief frees what has been unjustly confined. Knowledge of both construction and intrusion is therefore essential.

Dalt's followers are taught to understand the things they seek to open. A door should be strong enough to protect its rightful contents, a lock clever enough to deter those without legitimate access, and a key kept secure from those who would misuse it. Yet when a door protects tyranny, imprisonment, or stolen wealth, opening it may be the greater good.

### The Priesthood

Dalt's priesthood embraces an unusual combination of craftsmanship and thievery. His priests may spend one day designing a stronger door or more intricate lock and the next finding a way to defeat the same device. Workshops and temples often contain both carpentry and metalworking tools alongside lockpicks, keys, ropes, and other equipment used to bypass obstacles.

Dalt's priests commonly guard valuable objects, maintain secure vaults, and advise others on the protection of property. At the same time, they believe that valuables held by those who have no rightful claim upon them may properly be liberated. This distinction is left to the priest's judgment, and Dalt's followers are expected to develop both the practical skill and moral judgment necessary to make it.

The priesthood also preserves knowledge concerning portals and magical means of crossing boundaries. Some priests devote themselves to searching for forgotten gates, lost passages, and ancient keys.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Dalt is expected to:

- understand the construction and operation of doors, locks, keys, and other barriers;
    
- protect valuables entrusted to their care;
    
- maintain and improve useful means of securing property;
    
- learn methods of opening or bypassing barriers;
    
- free those who have been unjustly imprisoned whenever practical;
    
- recover objects improperly confined or withheld from their rightful owners;
    
- preserve knowledge of magical portals and passages;
    
- seek information concerning the imprisonment of Vatun;
    
- aid others in opening legitimate paths while preventing dangerous or malicious intrusions.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Dalt's priests are natural explorers, dungeon delvers, locksmiths, treasure guardians, and specialists in magical portals. They may join an expedition to recover a lost key, investigate an ancient gateway, bypass an otherwise impassable barrier, or recover property from those who have no legitimate claim to it.

Those who are aware of Dalt's connection to Vatun may also undertake journeys in search of artifacts, ancient lore, or magical knowledge that might reveal a means of breaking the enchantment that imprisons his brother.

### Priest of Dalt

**Ability Requirements:** Dexterity 15

**Alignment:** Chaotic Good

**Weapons:** As thief

**Armor:** As thief

**Major Spheres:** All, Astral, Divination, Guardian, Healing, Summoning, Wards

**Minor Spheres:** Creation, Elemental (All), Protection

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Magical Items:** Priests of Dalt may use magical items normally restricted to thieves when the item relates to locks, doors, traps, or movement through enclosed spaces, subject to the normal restrictions of the item.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Hold Portal:** The priest may cast _hold portal_ as a granted power.

**3rd Level — Opening and Sealing:** The priest may cast either _knock_ or _wizard lock_ as a granted power. The choice may be made each time the power is used.

**3rd Level — Rope Trick:** The priest may cast _rope trick_ as a granted power.

**5th Level — Leomund's Tiny Hut:** The priest may cast _Leomund's tiny hut_ as a granted power.

**7th Level — Hidden Shelter:** The priest may cast either _dimension door_ or _Leomund's secure shelter_ as a granted power. The choice may be made each time the power is used.

**10th Level — Leomund's Secret Chest:** The priest may cast _Leomund's secret chest_ as a granted power.

**13th Level — Guards and Wards:** The priest may cast _guards and wards_ as a granted power.

**Lockpicking:** A priest of Dalt can open locks as a thief of the priest's level, using the values given for thieves in the appropriate lockpicking table. This ability is part of the priest's normal training and does not require a separate proficiency.

**Opening and Closing Magic:** A priest of Dalt may cast any priest spell whose primary function is to open, close, seal, unlock, or create a portal, regardless of the sphere to which the spell normally belongs, provided the spell is otherwise available to priests.

## Damaran

**Titles:** Lord of Vermin, The Coward, The Survivor

**Alignment:** Neutral Evil

**Pantheon:** Touv

**Portfolio:** Vermin, Cowardice, Survival

**Symbol:** A rat surrounded by crawling insects

Damaran is the god of vermin and other creeping things, and of the instinct to flee from danger that allows living things to survive. He is the son of Meyanok and unquestioningly obeys his father. His older sister Vara can likewise browbeat him into obedience, although Damaran's usual response to danger is to flee rather than confront it.

Damaran is depicted as a strong Touv man with a skulking expression, usually accompanied by rats, insects, and other vermin.

#### Dogma

Survival is more important than pride. A dead hero accomplishes nothing, while a coward who escapes may live to fight another day. Damaran teaches his followers to recognize danger, avoid unnecessary risks, and make use of whatever resources are available, however unpleasant they may be.

Nothing living is truly without value. Vermin survive in ruins, sewers, swamps, and wastelands where other creatures perish. The wise learn from them. Food may be found where others refuse to look, shelter may be made from whatever remains, and even the smallest creature can become dangerous in sufficient numbers.

Damaran does not teach his followers to be helpless. He teaches them to survive when strength, wealth, and civilized comforts have failed. Those who threaten his followers should learn that creatures driven into a corner may become desperate and dangerous.

### The Priesthood

The priests and shamans of Damaran are survivors above all else. They know how to find food in places others consider barren or disgusting, locate shelter in hostile environments, and endure conditions that would drive ordinary people to despair. During famines, they are often among the most important members of their communities, teaching people how to find nourishment where none seems to exist.

Damaran's priests frequently serve reclusive Touv communities hidden deep within the jungles. Some deliberately establish settlements in places that other peoples consider uninhabitable, trusting in their ability to survive there.

They are also feared for their command of vermin. A Damaran priest may unleash rats, insects, or other creeping creatures against those who anger him, or call upon such creatures to aid another who has earned the faith's assistance.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Damaran is expected to:

- Preserve his own life and the lives of those entrusted to him.
    
- Avoid unnecessary risks and recognize when retreat is wiser than confrontation.
    
- Learn to find food, water, shelter, and useful materials in hostile environments.
    
- Aid communities during famine, disaster, and other times of scarcity.
    
- Protect reclusive settlements and those who cannot safely live among larger communities.
    
- Study vermin and other creatures that thrive in difficult conditions.
    
- Never waste a resource merely because it is unpleasant or beneath civilized use.
    
- Teach others methods of survival and escape.
    
- Punish those who deliberately threaten the survival of Damaran's people.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Damaran's priests adventure for practical reasons. They may seek new sources of food, water, medicine, or shelter; guide expeditions through regions where others would perish; or search for isolated Touv communities in need of aid. Others accompany adventurers because they recognize the value of learning how to survive unfamiliar dangers.

A Damaran priest rarely seeks a fair fight. Ambushes, traps, escape routes, summoned vermin, and overwhelming numbers are all preferable to a contest of strength. To retreat from an unwinnable battle is not shameful; to die needlessly is.

### Priest of Damaran

**Ability Requirements:** Dexterity 13, Constitution 14

**Alignment:** Neutral Evil or Neutral

**Weapons:** Atlatl, club, dagger, javelin, short bow, staff

**Armor:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Animal, Combat, Divination, Elemental (Water, Earth), Guardian, Healing, Necromantic, Protection, Summoning, Sun, Travelers, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Elemental (Water, Earth), Guardian, Necromantic, Protection, Sun, Weather

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** A priest of Damaran must wear ribbons or bands of black metal around the arms, neck, and legs.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Vermin's Kin:** The priest is never attacked by normal vermin unless the creatures are magically compelled or directly threatened by the priest or the priest's companions. The priest may also use _invisibility to animals_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Speak with Animals:** The priest may cast _speak with animals_ once per day.

**5th Level — Verminous Summoning:** The priest may cast _monster summoning I_ once per day, but may summon only vermin or other suitable small creeping creatures.

**7th Level — Giant Vermin:** The priest may cast _giant insect_ once per day.

**10th Level — Survival Through Fear:** The priest may cast either _animal growth_, affecting only vermin, or _emotion: fear_ once per day.

**13th Level — Creeping Doom:** The priest may cast _creeping doom_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** A priest of Damaran cannot turn undead.

## Delleb

**Titles:** The God of Sages, The God Savant

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Reason, Intellect, Knowledge, Learning

**Symbol:** An open book

Delleb is the god of sages and the pursuit of knowledge. He teaches that the mind should be cultivated to its fullest potential and that knowledge should be freely given as well as freely received. He is renowned for his honesty, neither lying nor resorting to half-truths, and is a patron of scholars, teachers, librarians, physicians, and all who seek to understand the world through reason.

Delleb delights in riddles, puzzles, and intellectual challenges. His long-standing enmity with Syrul, goddess of lies and deceit, reflects the opposition between honest inquiry and deliberate falsehood. Though Delleb's intellect is formidable, he encourages his followers to solve problems through their own reasoning rather than relying upon divine intervention.

#### Dogma

Knowledge is among the greatest goods, and the cultivation of the mind is a worthy pursuit in its own right. Learn all that you can, question what you are told, and test your conclusions against reason and evidence. Do not accept ignorance when understanding can be gained through study, nor mistake tradition or authority for proof.

Knowledge should be shared. Teach others what you have learned, preserve the discoveries of those who came before, and make learning accessible to all who sincerely seek it. Concealing knowledge for personal advantage is a betrayal of the intellectual gifts entrusted to you.

Truth must be pursued honestly. Lies, deliberate deception, and the manipulation of ignorance undermine the foundations of understanding. Debate and disagreement are valuable when they test ideas and expose errors; they become corrupt when their purpose is merely to mislead or humiliate.

Knowledge also brings responsibility. The pursuit of understanding does not supersede the sanctity of life, and learning should not be used carelessly to cause suffering. The church therefore debates continually how best to preserve dangerous knowledge without betraying its commitment to the free exchange of ideas.

### The Priesthood

Delleb's church is widespread throughout the lands settled by the Oeridian migrations, with particularly strong establishments in Perrenland, Veluna, Nyrond, the Ulek States, and Keoland. Its temples are often substantial structures of stone and timber, protected by surrounding walls and containing extensive libraries, classrooms, and facilities for scholarly research.

Many temples operate schools that provide free instruction to children and adults. Their libraries preserve books, manuscripts, legal records, scientific observations, and magical lore. Works are carefully indexed, cross-referenced, copied, and distributed to other temples or made available to those who wish to acquire them. The church regards literacy and education as essential to an informed society.

The clergy is divided into several traditions, most notably the scholars, librarians, and Knights of the Book.

**Scholars** are the largest group. They include philosophers, mathematicians, historians, physicians, natural philosophers, and sages who specialize in particular fields of knowledge. Some pursue learning for its own sake, while others conduct experiments or investigate questions about the world. Within this tradition, the teachers are responsible for spreading knowledge through temple schools, public instruction, and journeys to communities without established schools.

**Librarians** preserve and organize the written knowledge entrusted to the church. Their work includes copying manuscripts, maintaining catalogues, correcting errors, and preparing texts for circulation. They regard the accurate preservation of a work as a serious responsibility, since a careless transcription may mislead generations of future readers.

**The Knights of the Book** are a martial order devoted to protecting the church, its institutions, and the communities around them. They defend temples and libraries, serve as military advisers, and teach military history and strategy. They recognize that learning cannot flourish where scholars and students are constantly threatened by violence, though their willingness to use force distinguishes them from some of the church's more contemplative members.

The church also maintains hospitals and supports the study of medicine. Its physicians seek to understand illness through observation and investigation as well as through healing magic. The church's commitment to research can lead to uncomfortable ethical questions, particularly when the dead are examined to advance medical knowledge. Such practices are defended by some as necessary to prevent future suffering and criticized by others as an affront to the dignity of the deceased.

Dogs are highly regarded within the faith as intelligent, honest, and loyal companions. Many temples shelter strays, and black Labrador retrievers are especially favoured.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Delleb is expected to:

- cultivate the intellect through study, reason, observation, and debate;
    
- pursue knowledge honestly and distinguish established facts from conjecture;
    
- teach others and make learning accessible to those willing to pursue it;
    
- preserve books, manuscripts, records, and other sources of knowledge;
    
- support literacy, education, and the accurate transmission of information;
    
- investigate unfamiliar phenomena and test theories rather than accepting unsupported claims;
    
- oppose deliberate falsehood, fraud, and the use of ignorance to manipulate others;
    
- protect libraries, schools, scholars, and other institutions of learning;
    
- support the advancement of medicine and other disciplines that can alleviate suffering;
    
- respect the lives and dignity of others, recognizing that the pursuit of knowledge does not justify every possible means;
    
- consider carefully whether dangerous knowledge should be restricted, preserved under safeguards, or made freely available;
    
- engage in intellectual challenges, riddles, and debates that sharpen understanding.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Delleb's priests adventure to recover lost knowledge, investigate mysteries, protect scholars, and discover facts that cannot be learned from existing records.
