Some seek ancient libraries, forgotten ruins, or the remnants of lost civilizations. Others accompany expeditions to study unfamiliar creatures, magical phenomena, or distant cultures.

The church's commitment to knowledge also places its priests in conflict with those who deliberately destroy books, conceal discoveries for personal gain, or spread falsehood to control others. Knights of the Book may accompany adventurers to protect them from such threats, while scholars may join expeditions to document their findings.

An adventuring priest of Delleb is encouraged to think independently, question assumptions, and record discoveries carefully. A conclusion reached through sound reasoning is more valuable than an answer accepted merely because a powerful authority has declared it true.

### Priest of Delleb

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 15, Intelligence 16

**Prime Requisites:** Wisdom, Intelligence

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Weapons:** Any

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Combat, Divination, Healing, Law, Numbers, Thought, Time

**Minor Spheres:** War

**Granted Powers**

**Specialist Knowledge:** Each priest of Delleb may specialize in one nonweapon proficiency and develops exceptional expertise in that field. Proficiency checks using the chosen proficiency are treated as having an ability score of 19, regardless of the priest's actual score.

**Academic Insight:** Priests of Delleb receive the same bonuses to Intelligence- and Wisdom-based proficiency checks as the academician kit described in the _Complete Wizard's Handbook_.

**1st Level — Zone of Truth:** The priest may cast _zone of truth_ once per day.

**6th Level — Confusion:** The priest may cast _confusion_ once per day.

**10th Level — True Seeing:** The priest may cast _true seeing_ once per day.

**14th Level — Divine Inspiration:** The priest may invoke divine inspiration once per day.

**Turn Undead:** As a cleric.

## Ehlonna

**Titles:** Lady of the Forests, Huntress, Woman of the Woods

**Alignment:** NG

**Portfolio:** Woodlands, Hunting, Fertility, Protection, Elves and Half-Elves

**Symbol:** A unicorn's horn

Ehlonna is the huntress and woman of the woods, concerned with the protection and preservation of woodland life. She is particularly associated with good humans and demihumans who live in or depend upon the forests, and is a special protector of elves and half-elves.

Ehlonna is depicted as a young and beautiful woman who may appear human, elven, or half-elven, choosing whichever form she desires. In every form she is recognizable by her startlingly deep blue-violet eyes and exceptionally clear, fair complexion.

Kind and benevolent, Ehlonna nevertheless has little tolerance for those who despoil the wilderness. Evil humanoids, marauders, and others who destroy woodland communities or exploit the forest without regard for its life are among her particular enemies.

Her faith is especially strong among the Highfolk, among whom she is the most widely revered Power, and among wood elves. She is also worshipped throughout the forests of Ulek, Celene, the Suss, the Welkwood, and the Gnarley, as well as by scattered woodland communities elsewhere.

Services are generally informal and communal. Worshippers share simple repasts, often consisting of uncooked natural foods gathered from the surrounding woodland, drink wine, and play flutes, pipes, and lyres. Music and celebration are welcome expressions of her faith.

#### Dogma

The woodland is not merely a resource to be exploited but a living community worthy of protection. Hunt only what is needed, respect the creatures and plants that sustain life, and defend the wild from those who would despoil it.

Ehlonna's faithful are expected to protect those who dwell within the forest in harmony with it, regardless of whether they are human, elf, half-elf, or another good people. The strong should defend the vulnerable, and those who know the forest should use that knowledge to protect it.

Hunting is not inherently opposed to nature; waste and needless destruction are. The forest provides for those who respect it and resists those who treat it as something that exists solely for their profit.

### The Priesthood

Ehlonna's priests are accomplished woodsmen, hunters, scouts, and woodland spies. Men and women are equally represented, and the priesthood is organized through loose hierarchies rather than rigid ranks.

Priests commonly serve as protectors of woodland settlements and elven communities, guides through the wilderness, and watchers who identify threats before they reach the forest's inhabitants. They are expected to know the land intimately and to be capable of surviving without civilized comforts.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Ehlonna is expected to:

- Protect woodlands and their inhabitants from evil and needless destruction.
    
- Defend elves, half-elves, humans, and other good peoples who live in harmony with the wilderness.
    
- Hunt responsibly and avoid wasteful killing.
    
- Protect animals, plants, and other woodland life from needless destruction.
    
- Watch for marauders, poachers, loggers, monsters, and other threats to the forest.
    
- Guide travellers who respect the woodland and oppose those who would exploit it.
    
- Maintain knowledge of woodland paths, resources, creatures, and dangers.
    
- Teach others to live from the wilderness without destroying it.
    
- Preserve woodland sanctuaries and sacred places.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Ehlonna's priests adventure as scouts, guides, hunters, and protectors. They may seek out creatures threatening a woodland, investigate reports of logging or poaching, aid isolated communities, or oppose evil humanoids establishing themselves within the forest. Others travel beyond the woods to defend elves and half-elves or to recover objects and knowledge important to woodland communities.

### Priest of Ehlonna

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 13 or Dexterity 13 or Charisma 13

**Alignment:** Any good

**Weapons:** Dagger, knife, long bow and arrows, long sword, spear, staff, staff-sling, sling

**Armour:** Leather, padded leather, or elven chain

**Major Spheres:** Animal, Charm, Elemental (air, earth, water), Healing, Plant, Sun, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Creation, Guardian, Necromantic, Protection

**Special Spell:** _Stalk_

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Woodland Tracker:** The priest gains the Tracking proficiency.

**5th Level — Woodland Movement:** The priest may move silently in woodland terrain as a ranger of the same level.

**7th Level — Woodland Hide:** The priest may hide in woodland terrain as though using the ranger's ability to hide in shadows, with the same chance of success as a ranger of the same level.

**Turn Undead:** Turn at –3 levels.

## Erythnul

**Titles:** The Many, the Hateful, the Bloody

**Alignment:** Chaotic Evil

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Malice, Envy, Hatred, Panic, Slaughter

**Symbol:** A blood-red face with a bestial grin

Erythnul is the power of capricious malice, envy, hatred, and fearful panic. He delights in cruelty for its own sake, in the terror of the hunted, and in the rout of armies. His worshippers include humans and evil humanoids alike; gnolls, bugbears, and ogres are especially numerous among his devotees. He appears as a hulking, hairy brute with a red face and mad, staring green eyes, wielding a massive stone-headed mace. Erythnul can change his shape among human, gnoll, bugbear, ogre, and troll forms. He is also a summoner of creatures of battle, and legends claim that monsters spring from his blood when he is wounded.

#### Dogma

Hatred is a strength, envy a virtue, and mercy a weakness. The strong take what they desire, and the weak exist to be broken, humiliated, or driven before them in terror. Treachery is no sin when it serves one's ambition, and no bond of loyalty is sacred when betrayal offers an advantage. Erythnul's followers are encouraged to indulge their worst impulses, sow discord among their enemies, and take pleasure in the fear and suffering of others. Victory is welcome, but the panic and slaughter that accompany it are offerings in themselves.

### The Priesthood

Erythnul's clergy are cruel, volatile, and frequently divided against themselves. There is no fixed hierarchy binding the priesthood together. A priest commands only so long as others fear or respect his strength; a subordinate may establish his own authority by killing a superior. Envy and rivalry are actively encouraged, and priests are as likely to betray one another as to cooperate against outsiders.

Many priests are gnolls, bugbears, or ogres, though humans and other evil humanoids also serve the Many. Their ceremonies feature shrill reed instruments, clanging gongs, and pounding drums intended to unsettle and terrify. Major rites include burnt offerings. Rust-coloured garments are customary, while ceremonial robes are stained with blood.

Erythnul's worship is common among evil humanoids of the Bone March and the Pomarj, as well as among cruel bandits, brigands, and other violent bands throughout the Flanaess. His temples and shrines tend to be crude, fortified, and decorated with trophies taken from victims and defeated enemies.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Erythnul is expected to:

- Encourage hatred, envy, cruelty, and wanton violence.
    
- Exploit fear and confusion to scatter enemies and break their will.
    
- Take advantage of weakness, betrayal, and discord whenever they offer an opportunity.
    
- Offer blood and burnt sacrifices in the Many's honour.
    
- Participate in rites of drumming, gongs, and discordant reed music.
    
- Demonstrate personal strength and punish challenges to their authority.
    
- Never allow a rival's success to pass without considering how it might be turned to their own advantage.
    
- Spread Erythnul's influence among violent humanoids, raiders, and other potential devotees.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Erythnul's priests adventure to spread terror, lead raids, acquire power through violence, and indulge their appetite for destruction. They may serve as war leaders among humanoid tribes or attach themselves to bands of marauders, mercenaries, and brigands. Cooperation is usually temporary and self-interested. A priest may abandon allies when circumstances turn against them, or turn on a companion whose growing influence threatens his own position. Even within an adventuring company, the priest seeks opportunities to intimidate, dominate, and undermine those he considers rivals.

### Priest of Erythnul

**Ability Requirements:** Strength 14 or Constitution 14

**Prime Requisites:** Strength, Constitution

**Alignment:** Chaotic evil or neutral evil

**Weapons:** Any; mace is the favoured weapon

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** Combat, Creation*, Healing (reversed), Necromantic (reversed), Summoning  
**Minor Spheres:** Protection*, Sun (reversed), War*

**Granted Powers**

**4th Level — Fear:** The priest gains the ability to cast _fear_ once per day.

**7th Level — Strength:** The priest can cast _strength_ once per day, with the effect determined as described in the wizard spell of that name.

**9th Level — Sword of Wounding:** Once per day, the priest can enchant an edged weapon for one round per level, causing it to function as a _sword of wounding_ for the duration.

**Turn Undead:** Commands undead as a priest four levels lower than his actual level.

## Fharlanghn

**Titles:** The Dweller on the Horizon, the Far Wanderer

**Alignment:** Neutral Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Roads, Travel, Distance, Horizons

**Symbol:** A wooden disc inlaid with jade and turquoise, bearing a golden sun-disc

Fharlanghn is the power of travel, distance, roads, and horizons. His feet are ever restless, wandering the many worlds of the Prime Material Plane, while his eyes remain fixed on the horizon. He is depicted as a middle-aged man with weathered, wrinkled brown skin and bright green eyes, dressed in plain clothes and carrying little beyond an iron-shod staff and his distinctive disc. Made from many kinds of wood and inlaid with jade and turquoise, the disc represents the horizon and is also used for divination.

Travel throughout much of the Flanaess is perilous, and many who do not regularly worship Fharlanghn still leave him offerings before setting out on a journey. His priesthood is small and wandering, offering blessings and fellowship to those they meet on the road.

#### Dogma

The world is vast, and there is always another road to follow. Travel teaches what familiarity cannot: new lands reveal new customs, strangers may become friends, and every journey offers the chance to learn something of the world and oneself. The horizon is not a boundary but an invitation.

Travellers should help one another, share what they have learned, and offer hospitality to those in need. No road is entirely safe, but generosity and good sense can make its dangers easier to bear. A journey need not be undertaken for wealth or glory; the experience of travelling, the stories gathered along the way, and the people encountered are rewards in themselves.

### The Priesthood

Fharlanghn's priesthood is a small, loosely organized fellowship of wandering priests. Practical and good-natured, its members are often trusting to the point of naivety, yet their faith is sustained by the kindness they encounter on the road. They collect travellers' tales, record accounts of distant places, and sometimes draw or paint scenes from lands they have visited. Older priests who can no longer travel often settle in towns, where they preserve their accumulated knowledge and welcome passing wayfarers.

Services are held outdoors, preferably beneath a sunny sky. They commonly involve the telling of travellers' tales, the sharing of food and ale, and blessings for those about to depart or continuing their journeys. The atmosphere is informal and welcoming, with the road itself serving as a reminder that no single place contains all the world's wonders.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Fharlanghn is expected to:

- Travel regularly, visiting unfamiliar places and learning about the lands beyond familiar horizons.
    
- Offer practical aid, hospitality, and guidance to fellow travellers where possible.
    
- Share food and simple comforts with those in need.
    
- Collect and preserve tales, maps, observations, and knowledge of distant lands.
    
- Bless travellers before their journeys and offer thanks for safe arrivals.
    
- Respect local customs without assuming that unfamiliar ways are necessarily inferior.
    
- Help maintain roads, paths, bridges, and other works that make travel possible.
    
- Encourage others to explore the world, while warning them honestly of known dangers.
    
- Hold outdoor services and share travellers' stories, food, and drink in the god's honour.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring priests of Fharlanghn are natural wanderers. They may accompany caravans, guide expeditions, map unfamiliar regions, seek lost roads, or simply follow the horizon wherever it leads. They are often valuable companions because of their practical knowledge, willingness to help strangers, and familiarity with the hardships of travel. Although they are generally peaceful and trusting, they understand that the road can be dangerous and that kindness does not require foolishness. Their journeys also serve as opportunities to gather new stories and knowledge to share with other travellers.

### Priest of Fharlanghn

**Ability Requirements:** Standard

**Prime Requisites:** Standard

**Alignment:** Neutral good or true neutral

**Weapons:** Any non-edged weapon; staff is the favoured weapon

**Armour:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** Elemental (Air, Earth), Summoning, Travellers, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Combat*, Creation*, Healing*, Protection*

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Fleet of Foot:** The priest's movement rates are increased by 25%.

**3rd Level — Tireless Pace:** The priest gains a +3 bonus to ability checks made for jogging or running.

**5th Level — Dimension Door:** The priest can cast _dimension door_ once per day, as the 4th-level wizard spell.

**9th Level — Wind Walk:** The priest gains the ability to use _wind walk_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** Cannot turn undead.

## Fortubo

**The Stonefather, Keeper of the Forge, Guardian of the Stronghold**

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Portfolio:** Stone, metals, mountains, guardianship

**Symbol:** A hammer before a mountain

Fortubo is an ancient Suel god of stone, metal, craftsmanship, mountains, and guardianship. He was once a patron of the Suel people, but abandoned them in disgust after learning that the ancient Suel had created the derro through the enslavement and abuse of dwarves. Since then, Fortubo has shown little interest in restoring his former relationship with the Suel.

Most of Fortubo's worshippers are dwarves, although a smaller number of gnomes follow him. He is allied with Moradin and Berronar of the dwarven pantheon and strongly opposes theft, murder, evil, and the subjugation of dwarvenkind. He is commonly depicted as a small Suel man with a powerful build and features strongly resembling a dwarf; among dwarves, his resemblance to their own people is often made even more pronounced.

#### Dogma

What is built with care should be protected. What is entrusted to you should not be stolen, squandered, or abandoned. Strength exists to defend those who cannot defend themselves, not to dominate them.

Fortubo teaches that stone and metal endure because they are properly worked and maintained, and that communities endure for much the same reason. Craftsmanship requires patience, discipline, and respect for those who possess the knowledge to do a thing properly. Slavery and the deliberate destruction of another people are among the greatest affronts to his teachings.

### The Priesthood

Fortubo's priests serve as guardians of their communities. They protect settlements, workshops, mines, temples, and families, and frequently participate directly in construction and metalworking. His clergy includes both men and women without distinction. Married couples are particularly encouraged to enter the priesthood together, reflecting Fortubo's emphasis on loyalty, partnership, and the shared protection of the household.

Fortubo demands absolute devotion from his priests. A priest may not adopt another class after entering his service, nor may a priest combine his priesthood with another class. The restriction is regarded not as a punishment but as a sign that Fortubo's servants have accepted the responsibility of becoming guardians of their people.

No special holy weapon or ceremonial object is required. Any hammer may serve as a holy symbol, and many priests use the same hammer for both worship and practical work.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Fortubo is expected to:

- protect their community and those entrusted to their care;
    
- oppose theft, murder, slavery, and needless destruction;
    
- defend dwarves and others threatened with enslavement or extermination;
    
- maintain and improve structures placed under their protection;
    
- respect skilled craftsmen and preserve useful techniques;
    
- settle disputes where possible without bloodshed;
    
- ensure that weapons and tools are properly maintained;
    
- offer aid to those who are unable to protect themselves;
    
- refuse to abandon a legitimate responsibility merely because it has become difficult.
    

Children born to two priests of Fortubo are traditionally regarded as especially blessed. Such children are believed to inherit Fortubo's wisdom and endurance, and are said to possess exceptional Wisdom and Constitution from birth.

#### Adventuring Priests

Fortubo's priests adventure primarily when their responsibilities require them to leave the safety of their communities. They may escort caravans, defend mining settlements, recover stolen treasures, investigate threats to dwarven communities, or seek lost knowledge of stoneworking and metallurgy.

A priest may also undertake an expedition against slavers, derro, or other forces threatening dwarvenkind. Such an undertaking is considered particularly sacred when it protects a community that has no other means of defending itself.

### Priest of Fortubo

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 11, Constitution 12

**Alignment:** Lawful Good or Lawful Neutral

**Weapons:** Hammer (required), any other priest weapon

**Armor:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Combat, Creation, Divination, Elemental (Earth, Fire), Guardian, Healing, Protection, Wards

**Minor Spheres:** None

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Sanctuary:** The priest may cast _sanctuary_ as a granted power.

**3rd Level — Spiritual Hammer:** The priest may cast _spiritual hammer_ as a granted power.

**5th Level — Stone Shape:** The priest may cast _stone shape_ as a granted power.

**5th Level — Stone's Endurance:** The priest gains a +1 bonus to all saving throws against petrification. This bonus increases by +1 for every level gained thereafter.

**7th Level — Dig:** The priest may cast _dig_ as a granted power. When used to excavate or shape stone, the spell functions normally rather than being restricted to earth or loose soil.

**8th Level — Dwarven Restoration:** Dwarven priests of Fortubo may pray for _raise dead_ once per week. The spell may be used only upon dwarves or gnomes. Fortubo grants this power to only one dwarven high priest in the Flanaess at any given time.

**10th Level — Stone Barrier:** The priest may cast either _spike stones_ or _wall of stone_ as a granted power. The choice may be made each time the power is used.

**13th Level — Stone Tell:** The priest may cast _stone tell_ as a granted power.

**Hammer Mastery:** A priest of Fortubo gains a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with any stone or metal weapon. This is a standing benefit of the priesthood and is not limited to magical or specially consecrated weapons.

**Class Restriction:** A priest of Fortubo may not multiclass or dual-class. Abandoning Fortubo's priesthood in order to pursue another class is considered a serious breach of faith.

## Geshtai

**Daughter of the Oasis**

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Portfolio:** Rivers, lakes, springs, wells, fresh water

**Symbol:** A pool of water with an island at its center, upon which grows a palm tree

Geshtai is the goddess of the waters that sustain life. In the arid lands of the Baklunish West, where a spring or well may determine the survival of an entire community, she is among the most widely respected of the Baklunish gods. Farmers depend upon her for irrigation, nomads seek her guidance when crossing dry country, and travelers and riverfolk invoke her protection upon the water.

Geshtai is depicted as a young woman carrying a clay water jug. She is generally regarded as patient and compassionate, but her gifts are not to be squandered. Those who poison wells, destroy irrigation works, or deliberately waste water that others require commit serious offenses against her.

#### Dogma

Water is life. Preserve it, share it, and do not waste it.

Protect the springs, wells, rivers, and lakes upon which people and animals depend. Take what you require, but remember that water belongs to no single person forever. It flows from one place to another and sustains one life after another.

Do not allow thirst to become a weapon against the helpless. Aid those in genuine need, and preserve the sources that will sustain those who come after you.

### The Priesthood

The priesthood of Geshtai is particularly important among the nomadic Baklunish, where knowledge of hidden springs, seasonal watercourses, and reliable wells can mean the difference between life and death. Her priests also serve settled communities as guardians of wells and irrigation systems, advisers on agriculture, and protectors of rivers and lakes.

Geshtai's priests are expected to know the waters of the lands in which they live. Many maintain sacred springs or wells, and such places may serve as shrines, gathering places, and sites of religious ceremonies. Priests commonly bless new wells and sources of water and are called upon during droughts, floods, and disputes over water.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Geshtai is expected to:

- protect springs, wells, rivers, lakes, and other sources of fresh water;
    
- prevent the deliberate poisoning or destruction of water sources;
    
- maintain or supervise wells, irrigation works, and other essential waterworks;
    
- help travelers and communities locate safe sources of water;
    
- teach the proper conservation and use of water;
    
- aid those suffering from drought, flood, or thirst;
    
- avoid needless waste of fresh water;
    
- ensure that water is not deliberately withheld from those in genuine danger of death;
    
- preserve knowledge of local water sources for those who will follow.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Geshtai often accompany caravans and expeditions crossing dry country, or travel to investigate threatened water sources. Some seek lost springs, ancient wells, or forgotten irrigation works, while others intervene when drought, pollution, or deliberate destruction threatens a community.

A priest may also undertake long journeys to learn the waters of unfamiliar lands, believing that knowledge of where water may be found is among the most useful knowledge a servant of Geshtai can possess.

### Priest of Geshtai

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 11, Wisdom 12

**Alignment:** Neutral Good or Neutral

**Weapons:** Any priest weapon, spear, dagger

**Armor:** Any up to chain mail; no shield

**Major Spheres:** All, Animal, Creation, Divination, Elemental (Earth, Water), Healing, Plant, Protection, Travelers, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Necromantic

**Prohibited Spheres:** —

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Waterfinding:** The priest can identify pure, drinkable water with perfect accuracy by touch. The priest may also create water once per day, as the _create water_ spell.

**3rd Level — Locate Water:** Once per day, the priest may locate a source of naturally occurring water within 1 mile per level. This functions similarly to _locate object_, except that the priest need not have previously seen or handled the water being sought.

**5th Level — Water Walk:** Once per day, the priest may cast _water walk_ upon herself.

**7th Level — Part Water:** Once per day, the priest may cast _part water_.

**10th Level — Summon Water Elemental:** Once per week, the priest may summon a water elemental, using the rules for _summon fire elemental_ but summoning a water elemental instead.

## Heironeous

**Titles:** The Invincible, the Valorous Knight

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Honour, Justice, Chivalry, Valour, War

**Symbol:** A silver lightning bolt, often shown clenched in a fist

Heironeous is the champion of rightful combat and chivalrous deeds, patron of those who fight for honour, justice, and the fair and good order of things. He is depicted as a tall, youthful, exceptionally handsome man with coppery skin, auburn hair, and amber eyes, clad in chain mail and wielding a great magical battle axe. His skin is said to be magically enchanted to break most weapons that strike him, and the gifts granted him by the powers of lawful good make him an unmatched champion and protector.

Heironeous is widely revered throughout the nonevil lands of the Flanaess. Though traditionally favoured by officers, leaders, and paladins rather than common soldiers, his reputation as both a mighty warrior and a defender of the innocent has broadened his following.

#### Dogma

Heironeous teaches that honour must be demonstrated through action. Courage, justice, discipline, and mercy are not merely ideals to be professed, but obligations to be upheld even when doing so is difficult. Strength exists to defend the weak and uphold what is right, not to indulge cruelty or domination. Combat is justified when it serves a righteous cause, and victory achieved through dishonour is no true victory.

Those who wield authority must exercise it responsibly. Leaders should prepare carefully, command wisely, and accept responsibility for those placed in their care. A chivalrous warrior honours worthy opponents, protects those who cannot defend themselves, keeps sworn oaths, and refuses to let hatred overcome judgement. Evil and tyranny must be opposed with resolve, particularly the schemes and followers of Hextor, Heironeous's bitter rival.

### The Priesthood

Heironeous's priesthood is warlike and disciplined, with a strong military organization and effective systems of communication. Its ranks include many human priests who served as warriors before entering the clergy, as well as elven and half-elven fighter-clerics. Senior priests are respected for their strategic knowledge and serve as teachers to younger members.

The clergy maintains armories, trains for battle, and prepares to defend communities against threats. Priests are expected to oppose the clergy and agents of Hextor whenever their paths cross, though such opposition should serve the cause of justice rather than descend into indiscriminate vengeance. Possession of enchanted chain mail or a battle axe is a mark of distinction within the faith.

Temples are commonly imposing, finely decorated structures, often adorned with copper statues of Heironeous. Worship services feature triumphant battle hymns, offerings before the god's image, and the sharing of strengthening foods. Meat, full-bodied red wine taken in moderation, and spiced, stewed kara-fruk are traditional fare at important gatherings.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Heironeous is expected to:

- Uphold justice, honour, courage, and chivalrous conduct.
    
- Protect the innocent and defend those unable to defend themselves.
    
- Oppose tyranny, cruelty, and the abuse of power.
    
- Prepare carefully for battle and encourage discipline among those under their command.
    
- Honour legitimate oaths and accept responsibility for promises made.
    
- Treat worthy opponents with respect and reject needless cruelty.
    
- Train warriors and leaders in the responsible use of arms and authority.
    
- Maintain the readiness of the priesthood's armories and military organization.
    
- Oppose Hextor's clergy and their efforts to advance tyranny and evil.
    
- Offer prayers, battle hymns, and appropriate offerings in Heironeous's honour.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring priests of Heironeous serve as champions, protectors, military advisors, and leaders of righteous causes. They may accompany paladins and soldiers, defend settlements against aggression, expose corrupt rulers, or confront the servants of Hextor. They are expected to bring discipline and sound judgement to their companions, using force decisively when necessary but never treating violence as an end in itself. A priest's courage is measured not merely by willingness to face danger, but by the willingness to stand against wrongdoing even when victory seems uncertain.

### Priest of Heironeous

**Ability Requirements:** Strength 16, Dexterity 16, or Constitution 16

**Prime Requisites:** Strength, Dexterity, Constitution

**Alignment:** Lawful good

**Weapons:** Any; battle axe is the favoured weapon

**Armour:** Chain mail or plate armour only

**Major Spheres:** Combat, Guardian, Healing, Necromantic, Protection, War

**Minor Spheres:** Summoning*, Sun*, Wards*

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Courage:** The priest gains a +2 bonus to all saving throws against fear.

**4th Level — Cloak of Bravery:** The priest can cast _cloak of bravery_ once per day.

**6th Level — Resistance to Enfeeblement:** The priest becomes immune to magic that reduces Strength, including _ray of enfeeblement_ and similar effects.

**11th Level — Power Word, Stun:** The priest can cast _power word, stun_ once per day, as the 7th-level wizard spell.

**Turn Undead:** Turns undead as a priest two levels lower than his actual level.

## Hextor

**Titles:** Scourge of Battle, Champion of Evil

**Alignment:** Lawful Evil

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** War, Discord, Conflict, Tyranny, Physical Prowess

**Symbol:** Six arrows pointing outward in a fan

Hextor is the patron of evil warriors and those who seek power through violence, fear, and domination. He is portrayed as a six-armed man with grey skin, grey hair, and fiery red eyes, though he can assume a more handsome form when it suits his purposes. His many arms allow him to wield an array of weapons, and his priests aspire to emulate his mastery of combat.

Hextor's worshippers include evil warriors, assassins, mercenaries, and murderers. His following has long been strongest in the lands of the Great Kingdom, where his priests hold influence in many surviving fiefs and attend upon the tyrant Ivid. His greatest temples stand on sites marked by great battles and bloodshed. Worship services feature discordant wind instruments, shouting and screaming, and the clashing of iron weapons.

#### Dogma

Strength is the measure of worth, and power belongs to those capable of taking and holding it. Mercy invites contempt, weakness deserves subjugation, and order must be imposed through fear and force. A superior has the right to command those beneath him, while subordinates must obey so long as their masters retain the power to enforce obedience. Ambition is commendable when it leads to greater authority, but failure invites punishment.

War is not merely a means to an end; it is the proving ground in which the strong establish their right to rule. Discipline, cunning, and martial skill are essential, but they exist to secure domination rather than justice. Hextor's faithful seek to build hierarchies in which every individual knows their place and those at the top possess the strength to keep them there.

### The Priesthood

Hextor's priesthood is rigidly hierarchical, with authority concentrated in the hands of powerful and merciless superiors. Its priests are skilled combatants and assassins, combining direct violence with enough cunning to exploit an enemy's weakness. Subtlety is not their defining virtue, but neither are they mindless brutes: they value deception and tactical advantage when these contribute to victory.

The clergy maintains a strict chain of command, and advancement depends upon demonstrating strength, ruthlessness, and the ability to control others. Failure is punished, dissent is dangerous, and weakness is exploited. Unlike the followers of Erythnul, who thrive on uncontrolled malice and rivalry, Hextor's priests seek to channel cruelty into a disciplined instrument of conquest.

The clergy's vestments are black, adorned with white skulls or grey visages. Its ceremonies are harsh and martial, filled with discordant music, cries of aggression, and the striking of iron weapons. Temples are often fortified and imposing, especially those established on historic battlefields or sites of slaughter.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Hextor is expected to:

- Demonstrate strength, martial skill, and an unwavering will to dominate.
    
- Enforce obedience among subordinates and submit to legitimate superiors within the priesthood's hierarchy.
    
- Seek advancement through victory, discipline, and the defeat of rivals.
    
- Use fear, punishment, and violence to suppress resistance.
    
- Train warriors and assassins in effective methods of combat.
    
- Support campaigns of conquest and the establishment of tyrannical rule.
    
- Honour Hextor through martial ceremonies, the clashing of weapons, and other rites of conflict.
    
- Establish shrines and temples at sites associated with great battles or bloodshed.
    
- Oppose Heironeous and undermine those who champion his ideals of honour and just rule.
    

### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring priests of Hextor serve as military commanders, enforcers, assassins, and agents of conquest. They may lead troops in battle, train mercenaries, eliminate political rivals, or help establish a new regime through force. They favour companions who are useful, obedient, or powerful enough to merit respect, but regard uncontrolled disobedience as a threat to their authority. Even when cooperating with others, they seek to establish dominance and turn any success into an opportunity for greater power.

### Priest of Hextor

**Ability Requirements:** Strength 15 or Dexterity 15

**Prime Requisites:** Strength, Dexterity

**Alignment:** Lawful evil or neutral evil

**Weapons:** Any bow and its appropriate ammunition, flail, military fork, morningstar, scimitar, staff-sling

**Armour:** Chain mail or scale mail

**Required Proficiencies:** Religion (Oeridian), Weaponsmithing

**Major Spheres:** Combat, Elemental (Fire), Law, War

**Minor Spheres:** Healing (reversed), Necromantic (reversed), Summoning*, Sun* (reversed)

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Enhanced Strength:** The priest gains a +1 bonus to Strength.

**3rd Level — Two-Handed Weapon Mastery:** The priest can wield two-handed weapons without the usual attack-roll penalties associated with their use.

**5th Level — Ray of Enfeeblement:** The priest can cast _ray of enfeeblement_ once per day, as the 2nd-level wizard spell.

**9th Level — Devastating Melee:** Once per day, the priest can inflict double damage with melee attacks for one round per level.

**Turn Undead:** Cannot turn undead.

## Huhueteotl

**Titles:** The Fire God, Lord of the Passing Flame

**Alignment:** CE

**Pantheon:** Olman

**Portfolio:** Fire, destructive force, time

**Symbol:** A flame

Huhueteotl is the fearsome god of fire and the motion of time, embodying both the destructive nature of flame and the relentless passage of moments. He is always depicted as an armour-clad humanoid wreathed in fire, with a demonic reptilian head.

Huhueteotl demands many sacrifices from his followers and does not hesitate to punish those who displease him with bolts of flame. His worship teaches that time advances without mercy and that fire consumes whatever stands in its path.

#### Dogma

Time does not wait. Fire does not hesitate. Those who delay when action is required surrender their opportunity to those willing to act.

Huhueteotl's followers are taught to embrace decisive action, passion, and violent force. Fire destroys, but it also transforms; time destroys, but it also brings change. Neither can be stopped by pleading or hesitation.

Sacrifice is an essential part of Huhueteotl's worship. What is given to the god is consumed, and those who refuse what is demanded may themselves become fuel for his wrath.

### The Priesthood

The priests of Huhueteotl are warlike, hot-tempered, and inclined to act rather than wait. They frequently serve alongside or within the military, where their ability to rouse violent emotions in soldiers makes them valuable before and during battle.

They encourage aggression, courage, and decisive action, often deliberately inflaming the passions of warriors before combat. Their temper is not merely a personal failing but a reflection of the god they serve.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Huhueteotl is expected to:

- Maintain the sacrifices demanded by Huhueteotl.
    
- Preserve and tend the sacred fires of the faith.
    
- Encourage decisive action rather than hesitation when action is required.
    
- Rouse warriors to courage and violent purpose before battle.
    
- Serve and advise military leaders when called upon.
    
- Accept destruction and change as inevitable aspects of time and fire.
    
- Punish or sacrifice those who deliberately defy the demands of the god.
    
- Never allow fear of consequences to prevent necessary action.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Huhueteotl's priests often adventure alongside warriors, particularly when a military expedition or conflict has religious significance. Others seek ancient temples, sacred fires, or objects associated with the god.

A priest may also see an adventuring company as an opportunity to test the willingness of its members to act decisively. Hesitation in the face of danger is regarded as weakness, while bold action—even dangerous action—is worthy of respect.

### Priest of Huhueteotl

**Ability Requirements:** Constitution 15

**Alignment:** CE

**Weapons:** Atlatl, dagger, javelin, spear, sword

**Armor:** Any armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Charm, Combat, Elemental (fire), Guardian, Protection, Summoning, Sun, Time

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Healing, Necromantic

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Flame and Endurance:** The priest may cast _burning hands_ and _endure heat_ as granted spells.

**3rd Level — Flame or Withdrawal:** The priest may choose either _flame blade_ or _withdraw_ as a granted spell.

**5th Level — Haste or Flame Walk:** The priest may choose either _haste_ or _flame walk_ as a granted spell.

**7th Level — Courage or Fire:** The priest may choose either _emotion: courage_ or _fire shield (warm flames)_ as a granted spell.

**10th Level — Flame Strike:** The priest may cast _flame strike_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**13th Level — Fire Seeds:** The priest may cast _fire seeds_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**15th Level — Chariot of Sustarre:** The priest may cast _chariot of Sustarre_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**Turn Undead:** Turn

## Incabulos

**Titles:** The Black Rider, Bringer of Plagues, Power of Evil Sendings

**Alignment:** NE

**Portfolio:** Plague, Sickness, Drought, Famine, Nightmares, Despair

**Symbol:** A black sun or skeletal hand

Incabulos is the Power of evil sendings: plague, sickness, drought, famine, nightmares, and every other calamity that brings suffering to mortals. He is hideous in aspect, with skeletal hands, a deformed body, and a nightmarish visage. He is often depicted as a black-cloaked rider mounted upon a nightmare steed.

Incabulos has few true worshippers in the Flanaess, but many people seek to propitiate him with offerings when disaster strikes. His name is particularly feared in lands ravaged by famine, where disease spreads from unburied dead, or where chronic fear has taken hold of the population. Incabulos delights in being feared as much as revered.

#### Dogma

Suffering is inevitable, and Incabulos is its author. Disease, hunger, drought, fear, and despair are not merely misfortunes but manifestations of his power. His servants should therefore neither pity suffering nor attempt to prevent it when they can instead increase it.

Fear is itself a form of worship. Those who know that Incabulos is coming and tremble before him have already acknowledged his power.

### The Priesthood

The priests of Incabulos are intensely secretive. Even other evil priesthoods despise them, and their temples are hidden in subterranean chambers beneath forsaken settlements, plague-ridden lands, and other desolate places.

The priesthood maintains its secrecy through fear and threats. Senior priests are often paranoid, and some have been driven to insanity by their own obsession with betrayal and contamination. Juniors are expected to obey without question.

Incabulos's priests revel in suffering. They inflict disease and misfortune upon their victims, employ slow tortures, and take particular pleasure in the terror that precedes death or prolonged suffering. Their services are conducted in near darkness, accompanied by weird humming and droning chants and illuminated only by fat, smoky black candles. Followers celebrate acts of wickedness and pray for new evils to descend upon the world.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Incabulos is expected to:

- Preserve the secrecy of the priesthood and its places of worship.
    
- Obey senior priests without revealing the cult's activities to outsiders.
    
- Spread disease, suffering, fear, and misfortune whenever opportunity permits.
    
- Exploit famine, plague, war, and other disasters to increase Incabulos's influence.
    
- Inflict suffering upon captives rather than granting them quick deaths.
    
- Maintain the cult's rituals of darkness, chanting, and sacrifice.
    
- Encourage despair among those already afflicted by misfortune.
    
- Offer aid to no victim except when doing so serves to prolong or increase their suffering.
    
- Pray for further calamities and celebrate the suffering they bring.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Incabulos's priests venture into the world to spread disease and misfortune, establish hidden cults, exploit disasters, and locate communities vulnerable to despair. They may travel among plague victims disguised as healers, infiltrate other evil organizations, or deliberately carry sickness into healthy settlements.

Some are sent to recover diseased relics, forbidden texts, or magical objects associated with plague and death. Others simply seek opportunities to inflict suffering far from the scrutiny of their superiors.

### Priest of Incabulos

**Ability Requirements:** Not specified in the surviving source text

**Alignment:** Any evil

**Weapons:** Any; staff only until 1st level

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** Astral, Summoning

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Healing (Reverse), Necromantic (Reverse), Sun (Reverse)

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Special Spell:** _Plague_

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Hardened Against Corruption:** The priest has a 20% immunity to all diseases and slimes. This immunity increases by 2 percentage points for every level gained thereafter.

**5th Level — Hypnotic Gaze:** The priest may cast _hypnotism_ as a wizard of the appropriate level.

**6th Level — Contagion:** The priest may cast _contagion_ as a 4th-level wizard.

**7th Level — Enervation:** The priest may cast _enervation_ as a 4th-level wizard.

**8th Level — Deathly Slumber:** The priest may put one touched creature into a magical sleep. The effect is permanent until magically dispelled.

**Turn Undead:** Command

## Istus

**The Colorless and All-Colored, Lady of Fate, Weaver of the Web of Destiny**

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Portfolio:** Fate, destiny, predestination, divination

**Symbol:** A spindle and web

Istus is the Baklunish goddess of future destinies and predestination. She is not a goddess who grants good fortune so much as the power who determines what fortune will be. Her worshippers believe that the events of mortal life are threads within a vast and incomprehensible web, and that what appears to be chance is often merely the working of a pattern that mortals cannot perceive.

Istus has relatively few dedicated worshippers, but few Baklunish people are entirely indifferent to her. Her name is invoked in times of uncertainty, particularly when the consequences of a decision may affect an entire family, community, or nation. Offerings to Istus are especially common among those who have little confidence in their own ability to control what lies ahead.

She is depicted in three principal forms. As an **old crone**, she sits at a spindle and patiently spins the strands of the Web of Fate. As a **noble and haughty woman**, she represents Fate as an immutable power standing above mortal concerns. As a **cold young maiden**, she embodies the unknowable future: beautiful, distant, and untouched by the hopes or fears of those who seek to understand her.

Istus is neither benevolent nor malicious. Fate does not distinguish between those who deserve happiness and those who deserve suffering, and her priests are taught not to assume that it should. A favorable destiny may be a blessing, a trial, or merely the beginning of a greater misfortune. An apparent disaster may likewise prove to be the necessary beginning of something better. Istus does not promise that the future will be kind; she promises only that it will unfold.

#### Dogma

The future is a web, not a road. Every action creates consequences, but not every consequence can be understood by those who cause it. Some events may be avoided, others delayed, and still others brought about by the choices of mortals, but what is destined cannot ultimately be escaped.

Seek knowledge of what is to come, but do not mistake knowledge for control. A prophecy is a warning or a revelation, not a command. Those who learn their fate must still decide how they will face it.

Do not lie about what you know. The deliberate falsification of prophecy is an offense against Istus, particularly when done for personal advantage. Truth may be uncomfortable, incomplete, or misunderstood, but the priest of Istus must not knowingly replace it with a convenient falsehood.

Accept what cannot be changed. Resist what can be changed. Wisdom lies in knowing the difference.

### The Priesthood

The priesthood of Istus is small and generally respected, particularly by rulers, nobles, merchants, and others whose decisions may have consequences beyond their own lives. Priests are frequently consulted for divinations before wars, marriages, journeys, political decisions, and other events whose outcomes are uncertain.

Priests of Istus tend toward stoicism. They are trained to accept both good and bad fortune without allowing either to determine their judgment. Some become emotionally distant after years of contemplating the suffering and uncertainty revealed by their divinations, while others become unusually compassionate, believing that those whom Fate has treated kindly have an obligation to help those less fortunate.

Divination is central to the priesthood. Priests do not necessarily believe that every future event can be foreseen, nor that every prophecy has only one possible interpretation. Instead, they seek to distinguish genuine glimpses of destiny from the fears, hopes, and assumptions of those who seek their counsel.

Priests of Istus are expected to be scrupulously honest concerning their visions. Deliberately concealing a relevant truth from someone who has legitimately sought their counsel is considered a serious breach of duty, although a priest may refuse to perform a divination when revealing its subject would itself violate the will of Istus.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Istus is expected to:

- seek knowledge of fate through legitimate divination;
    
- speak truthfully about visions and omens;
    
- distinguish prophecy from personal opinion or speculation;
    
- counsel others without pretending to control their destiny;
    
- accept good and ill fortune with equal composure;
    
- avoid promising outcomes that cannot be known;
    
- preserve significant prophecies and records of fulfilled predictions;
    
- warn others when their actions appear likely to bring about a foreseen danger;
    
- respect the right of individuals to choose how they respond to their circumstances;
    
- oppose deliberate falsification or manipulation of prophecy for personal gain.
    

A priest is not required to accept every foretold event passively. If a vision reveals that a person will die tomorrow, the priest may attempt to prevent that death. Such an attempt is not necessarily contrary to Fate; the attempt itself may be one of the events revealed by the prophecy. The priest's duty is to understand and respond to destiny, not to assume that knowledge of the future makes action meaningless.

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Istus adventure to investigate prophecies, seek objects or individuals revealed through divination, accompany people whose destinies appear significant, or determine whether seemingly unrelated events form part of a larger pattern.

Some are sent to verify a prophecy whose meaning is disputed. Others deliberately seek situations in which the future is difficult to discern, believing that uncertainty itself may reveal something about the limits of mortal knowledge.

A priest of Istus rarely claims that an event _must_ happen merely because it has been foretold. Instead, they may regard the prophecy as another thread within the web, whose significance may only become apparent after the events surrounding it have unfolded.

### Priest of Istus

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 14, Wisdom 14

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Weapons:** Any non-edged weapon

**Armor:** Leather or chain mail

**Major Spheres:** Astral, Divination, Guardian, Healing, Necromantic, Numbers, Protection, Thought, Time

**Minor Spheres:** Charm

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Seer:** The priest casts all divination spells as though two levels higher for purposes of determining their range, duration, area of effect, and other level-dependent effects.

**3rd Level — Augury:** The priest may cast _augury_ once per day as a granted power.

**6th Level — Divination:** The priest may cast _divination_ once per day as a granted power.

**7th Level — Strand of Binding:** Once per day, the priest may create a strand of magical force that binds one creature within 40 yards. The target receives a saving throw vs. spell to resist. On a failed save, the creature is rendered immobile for 50 rounds, reduced by 2 rounds for each Hit Die or character level possessed by the creature, to a minimum of 1 round.

A bound creature may attempt to break the strand once during its imprisonment, using **half its normal Bend Bars/Lift Gates percentage**. A successful attempt frees the creature; otherwise the strand remains intact until its duration expires.

### Holy Days and Ceremonies

Istus has no single universally observed holy day. Her worshippers instead favor occasions associated with significant transitions: the beginning of a new year, coronations, marriages, the departure of armies, and the founding of settlements.

Offerings commonly consist of incense, candles, colored thread, and small objects representing something the worshipper hopes to preserve or something they fear losing. The thread is traditionally wound around a spindle or woven into a small web before being presented at the altar.

A person seeking an important divination is expected to provide an offering appropriate to the seriousness of the question. Priests do not normally set a fixed price for their services, as doing so would imply that Fate can be purchased.

### Priestly Vestments

Istus's priests traditionally wear gray or black robes decorated with web patterns. The patterns become increasingly elaborate with rank, and priests of 7th level or higher commonly wear robes embroidered with silver or white thread representing the strands of the Web of Fate.

The priesthood makes little distinction between male and female clergy. The three traditional forms of Istus are frequently incorporated into temple art, with the crone, noblewoman, and maiden representing the past, present, and future—or, according to some theologians, three different ways of perceiving the same eternal Fate.

## Iuz

**Titles:** The Old One, Lord of Evil, The Old

**Alignment:** CE

**Portfolio:** Evil, Tyranny, Cruelty, Fear, Deception

**Symbol:** A grinning skull

Iuz is an ancient and exceptionally powerful evil Power whose dominion is built upon cruelty, fear, and oppression. He delights in the suffering of others and encourages his servants to compete in acts of brutality, rewarding those who demonstrate their usefulness and ruthlessness.

Iuz commonly appears either as a tall, red-skinned fiend with steely fingers or as a shrivelled old man. He is capable of assuming many forms. Unlike most Powers, Iuz can be slain while physically present upon the Prime Material Plane; if destroyed there, he is banished to the Abyss, where his hidden soul gem preserves him from final destruction.

#### Dogma

Power belongs to those willing to seize and exercise it. Mercy is weakness, fear is a weapon, and those who cannot defend themselves exist to serve those strong enough to dominate them. Iuz's servants are expected to impose his will without hesitation and to take pleasure in the suffering of those beneath them.

Cruelty should be conspicuous, not merely practical. A priest who defeats an enemy without humiliating or tormenting them has demonstrated restraint where none was required. Those who serve Iuz are therefore encouraged to compete in acts of intimidation, brutality, and trophy-taking, each seeking to demonstrate that they are more ruthless than their rivals.

### The Priesthood

The priesthood of Iuz is cruel, barbaric, and sadistic. Its members revel in cruelty and the infliction of fear upon those weaker than themselves, while constantly competing to outdo one another. Advancement brings greater authority and access to Iuz's favour, but also greater responsibility for maintaining his dominion.

Senior priests may serve as rulers of provinces within Iuz's domain, although such authority is a mixed blessing: Iuz holds them personally responsible for what occurs within their territories. The most exalted servants may be selected for the Boneheart, the inner hierarchy through which Iuz entrusts his most important servants with the administration of his realm. Membership in the Boneheart is an exceptional honour and is not an automatic consequence of priestly advancement.

Trophy hunting is an important part of the priesthood. Mounted heads, particularly those taken from powerful enemies, bring prestige. Possession of an extraordinary trophy—or a true work of art demonstrating exceptional cruelty—can bring considerable esteem among Iuz's followers.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Iuz is expected to:

- Enforce Iuz's authority through fear, cruelty, and violence.
    
- Humiliate and torment enemies and those who resist Iuz's rule.
    
- Compete with fellow priests for prestige and Iuz's favour.
    
- Gather trophies from defeated enemies, particularly noteworthy foes.
    
- Maintain order within any territory entrusted to their authority.
    
- Destroy or suppress opposition to Iuz and his servants.
    
- Demonstrate that they are willing to commit acts of cruelty that others would hesitate to perform.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Iuz adventure to extend their master's influence, gather trophies, hunt enemies of his realm, and acquire treasures or magical objects worthy of presenting to their lord. Others seek opportunities to distinguish themselves from rival priests through increasingly spectacular acts of violence and cruelty.

### Priest of Iuz

**Ability Requirements:** None

**Alignment:** Chaotic Evil or Neutral Evil

**Weapons:** Club, dagger, flail, knife, mace, sling, staff, staff-sling, two-handed sword

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** Chaos, Charm, Combat, Healing (reverse), Necromantic (reverse), Summoning

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Sun (reverse)

**Granted Powers**

**3rd Level — Change Self:** The priest may cast _change self_ as a 1st-level wizard.

**5th Level — Defiance of Good:** The priest gains a +2 bonus to saving throws against spells cast by good-aligned spellcasters.

**7th Level — Fear:** The priest may cast _fear_ as a 4th-level wizard.

**9th Level — Enervation:** The priest may cast _enervation_ as a 4th-level wizard.

**Turn Undead:** Command as a priest one level higher.

## Jascar

**The Lord of Hills, Mountain Father**

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Portfolio:** Hills, mountains, guardianship, good

**Symbol:** A silver mountain peak above a small hammer

Jascar is the god of hills and mountains and a steadfast champion of good. He is particularly concerned with protecting the highlands and the communities that depend upon them, as well as opposing the evil creatures that make their lairs beneath the earth. Phaulkon frequently joins him in his efforts against evil, while his brother Fortubo is both ally and friendly rival.

Jascar has a particular hatred for Beltar. To him, the caves, pits, and subterranean lairs favored by Beltar represent a corrupt inversion of the hills and mountains he protects. Jascar takes pride in shaping the land itself, forming hills from flatlands and mountains from hills, and often conceals treasures within them for worthy people to discover.

Jascar appears as a powerful man with long brown hair and a long brown beard, an unusual appearance for a Suel deity. He wears a thick silver breastplate and is commonly depicted carrying a hammer or mining pick.

#### Dogma

The mountains are strong because they endure. Good must likewise endure against those who seek to destroy or corrupt it. Protect the innocent, oppose evil, and do not permit those who dwell beneath the earth to prey upon those who live above it.

The land is not merely something to be conquered or exploited. Hills and mountains should be explored, understood, and protected. Hidden places may contain danger, but they may also conceal treasures, resources, and ancient things worth recovering. Those who find such gifts should use them responsibly and for worthy purposes.

### The Priesthood

Jascar's priests are guardians, explorers, miners, and hunters of subterranean evil. They study hills and mountains, map passes and valleys, investigate caves and other entrances to underground lairs, and lead expeditions into dangerous highland country.

His priests frequently organize mining surveys and prospecting expeditions, both to locate useful resources and to determine whether evil humanoids or monsters have established themselves nearby. When marauding humanoids threaten settlements or travelers, Jascar's priests may lead armed crusades against them.

Every priest chooses a **species enemy**, following the same principle used by rangers. The chosen enemy represents a particular species or kind of creature that the priest has sworn to oppose. The priest receives all normal benefits and penalties associated with that choice, and is expected to actively oppose the chosen enemy when doing so is consistent with Jascar's teachings.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Jascar is expected to:

- protect communities and travelers in hill and mountain country;
    
- oppose evil humanoids, monsters, and other threats to the innocent;
    
- explore and document caves, passes, valleys, and other highland terrain;
    
- investigate entrances to underground lairs;
    
- assist with legitimate mining and prospecting;
    
- oppose the enslavement or destruction of peaceful mining communities;
    
- maintain vigilance against the followers and creatures of Beltar;
    
- pursue their chosen species enemy when circumstances permit;
    
- use discoveries and recovered treasures for worthy purposes.
    

Jascar's priests commonly wear earth-toned clothing. During ceremonies they wear white cloth headdresses or caps of white metal, symbolizing the snow-covered peaks above the earth.

#### Adventuring Priests

Jascar's priests are well suited to expeditions into rugged country. They may join adventurers seeking lost mines, forgotten ruins, hidden treasures, or ancient mountain passes, or accompany expeditions intended to determine what creatures inhabit newly discovered caves.

Others adventure as crusaders against marauding humanoids or subterranean monsters. A priest may also deliberately seek out the lairs of Beltar's worshippers, regarding their destruction as both a sacred duty and a restoration of places corrupted by the Lady of Pits.

### Priest of Jascar

**Ability Requirements:** Strength 13, Constitution 13

**Alignment:** Lawful Good

**Weapons:** Any

**Armor:** Any metal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Combat, Divination, Elemental (Earth), Guardian, Healing, Protection

**Minor Spheres:** Elemental (Fire)

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Protection from Evil:** The priest may cast _protection from evil_ as a granted power.

**3rd Level — Meld into Stone:** The priest may cast _meld into stone_ as a granted power.

**5th Level — Resistance to Petrification:** The priest gains a +1 bonus to saving throws against petrification. This bonus increases by +1 for every level gained thereafter.

**7th Level — Warhorse Form:** The priest may assume the form of a heavy warhorse. While in this form, the priest cannot cast spells or use priest abilities requiring speech, hands, or other humanoid anatomy. The transformation otherwise functions as the priest's normal shapechange ability.

**10th Level — Pegasus Form:** The priest may assume the form of a pegasus. While in this form, the priest retains the ability to cast spells up to 4th level, provided that the spell requires no material component other than the priest's holy symbol. Other abilities requiring humanoid anatomy remain unavailable.

**13th Level — Earthshaping:** The priest may cast _move earth_ or _stone to flesh_ as granted powers. The choice may be made each time the power is used.

**Species Enemy:** At the beginning of the priest's career, the priest chooses a species of creature as a sworn enemy, following the ranger's rules for species enemies. The priest receives all benefits and penalties associated with that choice and is expected to oppose the chosen enemy when doing so does not conflict with Jascar's other duties.

**Turn Undead:** Priests of Jascar can turn undead as normal priests.

## Joramy

**Titles:** The Shrew

**Alignment:** Neutral (often tending toward Neutral Good)

**Pantheon:** Flan

**Portfolio:** Fire, Volcanoes, Purification, Creativity, Wrath

**Symbol:** A volcano; traditionally, a jacinth shaped like a flame.

Joramy is the goddess of fire and volcanoes, of passions that burn fiercely and convictions that cannot easily be subdued. Her origin is uncertain, but her worship is widespread throughout the Flanaess. She embodies fire's destructive power, its cleansing force, and its capacity to make way for new growth. The destruction of what has grown stagnant may be terrible, but from its ashes something new can emerge.

Joramy is hot-tempered, outspoken, and fond of argument. She challenges complacency, delights in vigorous disagreement, and rarely allows a rival opinion to go unanswered. Her quarrelsome disposition is an expression of her fiery nature rather than an end in itself. Though she can be harsh and quick to anger, she is generally good-natured and respects those who meet her passion with conviction of their own.

### Dogma

Let your passions burn fiercely, and never surrender your convictions merely to preserve an easy peace. Ideals and opinions shape a person; to abandon what one holds true simply because it is challenged is to betray oneself. Defend your beliefs with all the fervour you would bring against a physical threat, and do not mistake silence or indifference for wisdom.

Fire destroys, but it also purifies and renews. What has become corrupt, stagnant, or unfit may need to be consumed before anything better can take its place. Do not fear change merely because it brings destruction, but neither should destruction be mistaken for an end in itself. From the ashes of the old, new possibilities arise.

### The Priesthood

Joramy's priesthood is small and widely dispersed. All ordained priests of Joramy are women, although worshippers and lay adherents may be of either sex. Her priestesses are passionate advocates, protectors of their companions, and champions of causes they believe worthy. Many become political agitators, negotiators, or leaders of communities resisting oppression or entrenched authority. Others wander in search of new causes, new challenges, and new people whose convictions might be tested against their own.

A priestess ordinarily leads a wandering existence until she reaches 13th level, gathering companions who share in her travels and service to the goddess. These companions need not worship Joramy exclusively, but each is expected to tithe five per cent of all treasure acquired while travelling with her. The priestess holds these funds in trust for the establishment and maintenance of Joramy's holy places.

At 13th level, a priestess must establish a temple in an urban community, using the funds accumulated through these tithes. Alternatively, she may establish a sanctum in the wilderness, preferably near a volcano, geyser, or hot spring. A priestess who chooses the wilderness option must remain in the area until reaching 16th level, and may then resume wandering only if another priestess of at least 13th level takes her place. A priestess who establishes an urban temple must remain to oversee its development until the temple has a priestess of at least 13th level to succeed her.

Before reaching 21st level, a priestess must establish a wilderness sanctum if she has not already done so. A sanctum is a pilgrimage site and one of the holiest places in Joramy's worship. The founding priestess must ensure that it is properly staffed, and may not resume wandering until at least three priestesses of 13th level or higher reside there.

Priestesses are expected to maintain a broadly neutral outlook, though their protection of communities and promotion of civic life often incline them toward good. A priestess is sworn to protect anyone who worships or tithes to Joramy, regardless of alignment. This obligation is not easily set aside merely because the person protected holds opinions or allegiances the priestess opposes.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priestess of Joramy is expected to:

- Defend her convictions openly and passionately, and challenge complacency, injustice, and ideas she considers harmful.
    
- Protect those who worship or tithe to Joramy, including travelling companions who do not share her faith.
    
- Lead a wandering life during her early career, gathering companions and collecting the tithes needed to establish the goddess's holy places.
    
- Establish and maintain a temple or sanctum when her advancement requires it, and ensure that each holy place is properly staffed before departing.
    
- Conduct Joramy's rites around an open flame, accepting the customary risks as an expression of devotion.
    
- Abstain from casting spells that invoke cold, and bathe only in heated water.
    
- Honour fire as both a destructive and renewing force, never reducing its significance to destruction alone.
    

### Devotions and Rites

Joramy's rites always involve an open flame, commonly contained in an elaborate vessel. Before beginning a rite, a priestess passes her hands through the flames, risking injury as a demonstration of devotion. The customary saving throw is versus spell; failure inflicts 1d4 points of damage. Pipes and bells commonly provide musical accompaniment.

**The Rite of Passage** admits a new lay adherent into Joramy's worship. Every companion travelling with a priestess must undergo this rite before gaining two levels in her company. After receiving the priestess's blessing, each initiate leaps through a great bonfire, risking 1d10 points of damage on a failed saving throw versus spell. This damage must heal naturally. Seeking magical healing for injuries suffered in the rite is considered an affront to Joramy. Until the offender atones, all fire attacks inflict double damage upon them, with no saving throw permitted against that additional damage.

**The Rite of the Dancing Flame** is the principal communal celebration of Joramy's faith and is performed at night. Worshippers and those who tithe to the goddess gather around a fire, dancing at the edge of its circle of illumination. While the dance continues, the presiding priestess prepares a special heated beverage. As the dancers tire, she leads them from the circle one by one and gives each a portion of the drink, which restores their vitality and revives the exhausted. The rite concludes with the participants joining hands around the fire, offering a prayer to Joramy, and celebrating with feasting and revelry.

**The Rite of the Dying Light** is the cremation ceremony performed for a priestess of Joramy or one of her companions. The deceased is placed upon a raised platform above a funeral pyre. If Joramy wills it, the dead may return to life through a successful saving throw versus spell, normally at a −3 penalty. The penalty is reduced to −2 for a priestess of 13th level or higher, and to −1 for a priestess of 21st level or higher.

A participant may sacrifice one character level for a +1 bonus to this saving throw. For each level sacrificed, the character gains a cumulative +10% experience bonus and +2% magic resistance until the lost level is regained. Such sacrifice is regarded as an exceptional expression of devotion.

#### Adventuring Priests

Joramy's priestesses adventure to champion causes, challenge entrenched powers, and bring their convictions into contact with the wider world. They may support a threatened community, lead resistance against an oppressive ruler, mediate disputes that have grown dangerously heated, or seek out people whose beliefs they consider worthy of challenge. Some travel in search of new techniques, crafts, or ideas that might inspire creation after destruction.

Their companions are more than convenient allies: they are people whom the priestess has accepted into her protection. A priestess who gathers wealth through their adventures is expected to preserve the tithes entrusted to her rather than treating them as personal treasure.

### Priestess of Joramy

**Ability Requirements:** Standard.

**Alignment:** Neutral. A priestess may tend toward Neutral Good but must retain a fundamentally neutral outlook.

**Weapons:** Flail, short sword (poniard).

**Armour:** Chain mail.

**Major Spheres:** All, Chaos, Charm, Divination, Elemental (Fire), Guardian, Healing, War, Wards.

**Combat Spells:** _Prayer, Flame Strike, Spiritual Wrath,_ and _Holy/Unholy Word_.

**Granted Powers**

**Priesthood Restrictions:** A priestess of Joramy cannot cast spells that invoke cold. She must bathe only in heated water and is expected to observe the customary fire rites of her faith. Her clergy-specific obligations regarding companions, tithes, temples, and sanctums are described under The Priesthood.

**Turn Undead:** As a standard priest.

## Katay

**Titles:** The Keeper of Time, The Record Keeper, The Inevitable

**Alignment:** Lawful Neutral

**Pantheon:** Touv

**Portfolio:** Decay, Inevitability, Order, Time

**Symbol:** A copper wheel inscribed with Touv runes

Katay was born of Breeka without a father. He represents the relentless cycle of birth, growth, decay, and death within his mother's realm, as well as the passage of time that gradually transforms all living things. He is the inventor of the Touv calendar and records all significant events upon the great metallic wheel given to him by Xanag.

Katay is depicted as an elderly Touv man with youthful eyes. He wears a decaying animal pelt and carries a great copper disk covered with Touv runes.

#### Dogma

Everything has its time. Birth leads to growth, growth to decay, and decay to death, after which new life may arise. None of these stages is an error or injustice. To Katay's followers, the attempt to deny what must eventually happen is itself a form of disorder.

Katay teaches that time must be measured, remembered, and respected. Events should be recorded so that later generations can learn from them, obligations should be fulfilled when they become due, and laws should provide a stable order through which people may live. Individuals may struggle against their circumstances, but no one escapes time forever.

Decay is not evil. It is the natural consequence of age and the means by which the old makes room for the new. What matters is that the proper order of things be preserved and that the lessons of the past not be lost.

### The Priesthood

Katay's priests are the record keepers of the Touv people. They maintain calendars, genealogies, birth and death records, accounts of important events, and observations of weather and other recurring natural phenomena. Their records are intended not merely to preserve history but to provide knowledge that future generations can study and use.

Katay's priests preside over births and funerals, mark the passage of seasons and important dates, and maintain the rituals by which communities recognize the passage of time. They also serve as judges and legal authorities, upholding the laws and established customs of the people.

A Katayan priest is expected to be meticulous and impartial. Altering a record to serve a personal interest, deliberately obscuring an inconvenient truth, or manipulating the calendar for political advantage are serious offenses.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Katay is expected to:

- Maintain accurate records of births, deaths, marriages, important events, and natural phenomena.
    
- Preserve the history and traditions of the Touv people.
    
- Maintain and calculate the calendar and mark important dates and seasons.
    
- Preside over births, funerals, and other rites marking significant transitions.
    
- Uphold the laws and established customs of the community.
    
- Serve as an impartial judge or witness when called upon.
    
- Study the causes and consequences of events so that their lessons are not forgotten.
    
- Accept the natural processes of aging, decay, and death.
    
- Oppose deliberate falsification, needless disorder, and attempts to conceal what must eventually be known.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Katay's priests adventure to recover lost records, investigate events whose histories have been obscured, establish the correct date or chronology of ancient events, and preserve knowledge threatened by war or disaster. Some accompany expeditions because their records may be the only surviving account of what occurred.

Others travel as judges, legal representatives, or witnesses, particularly when disputes involve competing claims of ancestry, ownership, or ancient obligation. A Katayan priest may also seek objects or inscriptions whose age and history are themselves important to understanding the past.

### Priest of Katay

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 14

**Alignment:** Lawful Neutral

**Weapons:** Chakram, dagger, short bow, spear, staff

**Armor:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Charm, Divination, Elemental, Healing, Law, Necromantic, Time

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Guardian, Elemental, Protection

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** A priest of Katay must wear an old animal pelt and carry a copper disk suspended from a chain. The disk is normally engraved with Touv runes and is used as the priest's holy symbol.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Keeper of the Hours:** The priest may cast _know time_ at will. The priest may also _purify food and drink_ or _putrefy food and drink_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Withdrawal:** The priest may cast _withdraw_ once per day.

**5th Level — Between Life and Death:** The priest may cast either _feign death_ or _speak with dead_ once per day.

**7th Level — Unchanging:** The priest is permanently affected by _free action_, as though the spell were continuously active upon him.

**10th Level — Time Pool:** The priest may cast _time pool_ once per day.

**13th Level — The Inevitable End:** The priest may cast _slay living_ once per day. This power represents Katay bringing a creature's allotted time to its end; it cannot be reversed through Katay's priesthood by means of the reverse of _slay living_.

**15th Level — Wither:** The priest may cast _wither_ once per day. This is the reverse of _regenerate_ and causes the target's body to suffer the effects of extreme age and decay as described by the spell.

**Turn Undead:** A priest of Katay may turn undead normally.

## Kord
