Their architecture is deliberately deceptive: walls, carvings, paintings, and furnishings are designed to appear different when viewed from different angles or after prolonged observation.

Priests of Syrul favor golden robes embroidered with the symbol of the forked tongue, lined in brown or black. Their ceremonial staffs commonly conceal daggers. Adventuring priests wear yellow or gold garments marked with a recognizable red forked-tongue symbol, however dirty or ragged the clothing becomes.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Syrul is expected to:

- cultivate the ability to deceive and recognize deception;
    
- conceal the church's secrets through falsehood, omission, misdirection, or carefully chosen truths;
    
- maintain useful identities, relationships, and reputations;
    
- seek secrets and information that can be traded, exploited, or used against those who possess them;
    
- use trust as a resource rather than treating it as a virtue;
    
- study magical and mundane means of detecting deception and develop ways to resist them;
    
- employ promises when useful, but never allow an obligation to become more important than the interests of Syrul.
    

The eleventh day of each month is a holy day. On this day, priests are forbidden to knowingly speak a simple truth when a convincing falsehood can serve instead. Younger priests are generally expected to observe their elders and learn from their examples. The purpose is not merely to lie, but to demonstrate mastery of the distinction between truth, falsehood, implication, and omission.

#### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring priests commonly serve as spies, negotiators, infiltrators, investigators, or agents of political and commercial interests. Some seek information that can later be traded or weaponized; others deliberately insert themselves into organizations and communities to create opportunities for future betrayal.

Syrul's priests are particularly interested in secrets, hidden identities, compromised officials, and magical means of detecting deception. They often regard resistance to divination as both a practical necessity and a religious duty.

### Priest of Syrul

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 13, Wisdom 9

**Alignment:** Lawful Evil or Neutral Evil

**Weapons:** Crossbow, dagger, garrote, short sword, staff, whip

**Armor:** Any armor up to chain armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Astral, Charm, Divination, Healing, Summoning

**Minor Spheres:** Elemental, Guardian, Necromantic, Protection, Thought

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Magical Items:** As clerics

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Unmask Divination:** The priest automatically knows when a divination spell is being cast upon them. If the priest succeeds on a saving throw vs. spell, they identify the exact spell being used. The priest may also cast _friends_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Conceal Alignment:** The priest may cast _obscure alignment_ once per day.

**7th Level — Untruth Unseen:** The priest may cast _undetectable lie_ once per day.

**8th Level — Syrul's Answer:** Syrul grants the priest a vision without requiring a sacrifice. The priest's question is answered, subject to the nature and limits of divine knowledge. The priest also gains a +1 bonus to attack rolls when using one weapon chosen upon receiving this gift.

**13th Level — Expose Falsehood:** The priest may cast _detect lie_ once per day. This ability does not function against priests of Syrul.

**15th Level — Nightmare Steed:** Once per month, the priest may summon a nightmare as a steed. After an hour of prayer, the nightmare appears. It will serve the priest for up to 72 hours if offered the appropriate quantity of oat-shaped platinum flakes. If the offering is not made, the nightmare attacks the summoner instead.

### Names of Rank

Priests of 4th level or higher are called **Perjurers**, those of 6th level or higher are **Fabulists**, and those of 10th level or higher are **Equivocators**. The general term **Forktongue** applies to any member of Syrul's priesthood.

### Affiliated Orders

Rumors persist of a secret order of Syrul-worshipping assassins specializing in disguise, impersonation, and infiltration. Some claim that these agents have replaced important officials in foreign governments; others insist that the order does not exist. Both claims may be deliberate fabrications.

## Telchur

**Titles:** Father Frost, The Ice Man, The Frostbite Lord, Walker of the Wastes, Master of the North Wind

**Alignment:** CN

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Winter, Cold, North Wind

**Symbol:** Icicle

Telchur, first son of Velnius, was entrusted with the world while his father attended to other worlds in Greyspace. In his youth and arrogance, Telchur froze Oerth beneath an eternal winter. When Velnius returned, he created Telchur's three siblings and charged them with regulating the seasons alongside their brother. Telchur remains the angry, spoiled son who defied his father and never accepted his punishment.

As god of winter, Telchur brings the north wind, famine, and death. His early frosts destroy crops, and his bitter cold kills livestock and travellers with indifferent abandon. Yet winter also possesses a silent beauty that some genuinely love. Telchur is as unpredictable as the wind that bears his name: cruel and destructive one moment, beautiful and serene the next.

#### Dogma

Winter always returns, and the wise prepare for its coming. The faithful disagree on what preparation means. Some believe that people must gather firewood, preserve food, and secure shelter before the first cold winds arrive. Those who make no effort to prepare deserve whatever winter brings them.

Others believe that the proper response is to harden oneself against the cold. These devotees expose themselves to winter's worst conditions, seeking to overcome their weakness and become worthy of standing in Telchur's presence. Many suffer grievous injury or die in the attempt, and their fellow worshippers regard them as martyrs to the Ice Man.

Both traditions acknowledge winter's inevitability, but they differ on whether one should endure it through preparation or through personal endurance.

### The Priesthood

Telchur's church is strongest in the northern lands, particularly among the Wolf and Tiger Nomads. His worship also occurs among the barbarian peoples, though permanent temples are rare outside the North Province and Blackmoor.

The church contains two principal traditions. The more settled clergy seek to appease Telchur and protect their communities through preparation, preservation, and practical assistance. They maintain substantial temples built to withstand the cold, with insulated walls, heavy rugs, furs, blankets, and plentiful fireplaces. The other tradition embraces the cold itself. Its devotees favour natural shrines in caves, snowbanks, and windbreaks, where fires are forbidden and winter is encountered without shelter.

The shamans, commonly drawn from nomadic and barbarian peoples, are particularly associated with the latter tradition. They seek visions in falling snow and patterns of frost, and some deliberately expose themselves to dangerous conditions in pursuit of spiritual strength. The more established clerics generally serve the former tradition.

The Guardians of the Cold, Telchur's specialty priests, are a comparatively recent addition to the church. They reconcile the two traditions: their resistance to cold earns the respect of those who embrace winter, while their appreciation of warmth and their willingness to protect others make them welcome among the more settled clergy.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Telchur is expected to:

- Respect winter as an inevitable and recurring force, and prepare for its arrival.
    
- Gather firewood, preserve food, and ensure that suitable shelter is available when the cold approaches.
    
- Contemplate winter and seek understanding through its storms, snowfall, and frost.
    
- Attend to the sick, injured, and stranded during severe weather, when circumstances and personal conviction permit.
    
- Observe the seven days of the first week of Fireseek, the church's principal annual holy period.
    
- Honour blizzards as sacred manifestations of Telchur, whether by aiding those endangered by them or by enduring their fury.
    
- Recognize that the church contains both those who appease winter and those who embrace it, even when their practices differ.
    

Evil priests may interpret Telchur's demands more cruelly, seeking sacrifices among those lost in winter storms.

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Telchur travel to remote regions, seek out natural shrines, study unusual weather, and explore the frozen wastes. Some venture into storms to rescue the stranded; others seek visions or test their endurance against the elements. Their services are especially valuable in cold climates, where their knowledge of winter preparation and their devotion to the Ice Man can help communities survive.

The church has no affiliated orders. Its greatest surviving temple is the Frost Palace, a magnificent structure of white marble in Blackmoor, reputed to stand east of Dantredun near the Cold Marsh. The former Iceminster in the North Province was dismantled for firewood during the winter of 246 CY. Telchur's priests have also long sought the Hanging Glacier of Alisedran, believed to lie somewhere in the Corusk Mountains.

### Priest of Telchur

**Ability Requirements:** Constitution 17

**Prime Requisites:** Wisdom, Constitution

**Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral

**Weapons:** Any

**Armour:** Any; non-metal armour is preferred

**Major Spheres:** All, Combat, Elemental (Air), Necromantic, Protection, Sun, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Travellers

**Granted Powers**

**Cold Resistance:** Guardians of the Cold are immune to normal cold and gain a +1 bonus to saving throws against magical cold.

**4th Level — Gust of Wind:** The guardian can cast _gust of wind_ once per day.

**6th Level — Wall of Ice:** The guardian can cast _wall of ice_ once per day.

**8th Level — Ice Storm:** The guardian can cast _ice storm_ once per day.

**10th Level — Cone of Cold:** The guardian can cast _cone of cold_ once per day.

**15th Level — Uncontrollable Weather:** The guardian can cast _uncontrollable weather_ from the _Tome of Magic_ once per day, but only in warm weather. Creating warm conditions earns Telchur's displeasure.

**Turn Undead:** Good-aligned clerics, specialty priests, and shamans may turn undead. Evil-aligned clergy may command undead. Neutral-aligned clergy can do neither.

## Tezcatlipoca

**Titles:** The Smoking Mirror, Jaguar God

**Alignment:** CE

**Pantheon:** Olman

**Portfolio:** Sun, Moon, Night, Scheming, Betrayal, War, Wealth

**Symbol:** A smoking mirror or jaguar

Tezcatlipoca is a dual-aspect greater god who rules over the sun as well as its absence. He embodies both the light that nourishes the world and the darkness that conceals treachery. He and Quetzalcoatl are ancient rivals, and the servants of the Jaguar God never willingly support those of the Feathered Serpent.

Tezcatlipoca spreads disorder and war and teaches that wealth is power. He favours those who are clever enough to seize advantage from confusion and ruthless enough to exploit the weaknesses of others.

#### Dogma

Power belongs to those capable of taking and keeping it. Wealth, influence, cunning, and the ability to manipulate others are signs of strength, while those who allow themselves to be deceived deserve their fate.

Tezcatlipoca's followers are encouraged to create conflict rather than merely endure it. A rival weakened by intrigue is preferable to one defeated at great cost, and an enemy city divided against itself is already half-conquered. His priests honour both the sun that sustains their own fields and the darkness that allows them to ruin those of their enemies.

### The Priesthood

Tezcatlipoca's priests are politically active and ambitious. Like the priests of Quetzalcoatl, they seek influence over rulers and civic institutions, but they readily employ deception, blackmail, manipulation, betrayal, and other underhanded methods when straightforward action proves ineffective.

The priesthood performs sacrifices intended to secure gentle sunlight for its own crops while bringing parching heat upon the crops of enemies. The most important annual sacrifice is that of a perfect young man chosen to embody the god. He is pampered and honoured from midsummer to midsummer before his heart is sacrificed to Tezcatlipoca.

Priests actively encourage warfare between rival cities and seek opportunities to turn political disputes into open conflict.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Tezcatlipoca is expected to:

- Increase the influence and wealth of the priesthood through political means.
    
- Exploit rivalries, weaknesses, and divisions among enemies.
    
- Prefer deception and indirect action when they offer an advantage.
    
- Encourage warfare between rival cities and peoples.
    
- Offer sacrifices for favourable sunlight and against the crops of enemies.
    
- Maintain the annual rites surrounding the god's chosen sacrificial figure.
    
- Honour Tezcatlipoca through displays of wealth, power, and cunning.
    
- Never willingly aid the servants or institutions of Quetzalcoatl.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Tezcatlipoca's priests adventure as political agents, spies, emissaries, and instigators of conflict. They may seek wealth, ancient treasures, magical mirrors, or other objects that increase their influence. They are particularly interested in weakening rival cities, undermining enemy leaders, and exploiting divisions within communities.

### Priest of Tezcatlipoca

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 13, Dexterity 14

**Alignment:** CE, NE

**Weapons:** Atlatl, dagger, javelin, short bow, sword

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Astral, Combat, Divination, Healing, Protection, Sun, War

**Minor Spheres:** Animal, Charm, Creation, Elemental (Fire, Air), Necromantic

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Sun and Shadow:** The priest may cast _dancing lights_ or _light_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Read the Weakness:** The priest may cast _charm person_ or _ESP_ once per day.

**5th Level — Starshine:** The priest may cast _starshine_ or _suggestion_ once per day.

**7th Level — The Smoking Mirror:** The priest may cast _reflecting pool_. The spell functions normally, but the priest may also use mirrors as the reflecting surface.

**10th Level — Sun and Moon:** The priest may cast _moonbeam_ or _seeming_ once per day.

**13th Level — Deceiver's Mask:** The priest may cast _mislead_ or _speak with monsters_ once per day.

**15th Level — Path of Darkness:** The priest may cast _shadow walk_ or _sunray_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** Nil

## Tlaloc

**Titles:** Lord of Rain

**Alignment:** LE

**Pantheon:** Olman

**Portfolio:** Rain, Agriculture, Storms, Fertility

**Symbol:** A rain-filled vessel or white feather

Tlaloc is the intermediate god of rain, appearing as a black-clad reptilian humanoid with bulging eyes and enormous tusks. He is a relatively peaceful deity, concerned primarily with his own domain rather than the affairs of other gods. His favour brings rain and prosperous seasons; his anger brings blight, cold, rot, and the destruction of crops.

Tlaloc demands regular sacrifice in exchange for his favour. Children are sacrificed to him each month to ensure rain, while numerous annual sacrifices are made to encourage a prosperous growing season.

#### Dogma

Rain is a gift, not a right. Those who depend upon the land depend upon Tlaloc, and those who prosper from his favour owe him gratitude and obedience. Failure to honour him invites disaster.

Tlaloc's priests teach that the proper response to his blessings is sacrifice, prayer, and submission. His anger must be avoided at all costs, for the same power that nourishes the fields can bring blight, cold, and rot.

### The Priesthood

Tlaloc's priests maintain the sacrifices and rituals required to preserve their god's favour. They pray to him daily and oversee the sacrifices offered throughout the year.

The priesthood exercises considerable authority over agricultural communities. Priests remind farmers, herders, and others who depend upon the land that their livelihood rests upon Tlaloc's favour, and expect gratitude and obedience in return. They are generally not interested in conquest or political expansion beyond maintaining this authority and ensuring that Tlaloc's rites are observed.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Tlaloc is expected to:

- Pray to Tlaloc each day.
    
- Maintain the monthly sacrifices required to secure rain.
    
- Conduct the annual sacrifices intended to ensure a prosperous season.
    
- Ensure that Tlaloc receives proper offerings from those who depend upon the land.
    
- Demand gratitude and obedience from agricultural communities.
    
- Watch for signs that Tlaloc's favour is failing or his anger has been provoked.
    
- Protect sacred waters, agricultural lands, and places associated with Tlaloc's worship.
    
- Warn communities of the consequences of neglecting the god.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Tlaloc's priests adventure when drought, blight, or other threats to the land require divine intervention. They may seek sacred objects, investigate disturbances to natural weather patterns, or travel to communities that have neglected Tlaloc's worship. They may also accompany expeditions intended to secure fertile territory or sources of water.

### Priest of Tlaloc

**Ability Requirements:** Constitution 13

**Alignment:** LE

**Weapons:** Dagger, javelin, short bow, sword

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Astral, Elemental (Water), Healing, Plant, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Animal, Combat, Divination, Protection

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Gift of Rain:** The priest may cast _create water_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Veil of Mist:** The priest may cast _obscurement_ once per day.

**5th Level — Tlaloc's Wrath:** The priest may cast _call lightning_ once per day.

**7th Level — Storm and Fog:** The priest may cast _ice storm_ or _wall of fog_ once per day.

**10th Level — Waters of the Sky:** The priest may cast _rainbow_ or _transmute rock to mud_ once per day.

**13th Level — Summon the Rains:** The priest may cast _weather summoning_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** Nil

## Trithereon

**Titles:** The Summoner, the Pursuit of Freedom

**Alignment:** Chaotic Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Individuality, Liberty, Retribution, Self-Protection

**Symbol:** A pursuit rune

Trithereon is the power of individuality and the right to defend oneself against those who would abridge life or freedom. His pursuit rune represents the struggle for liberty and the determination to bring down those who would deny it to others.

Trithereon appears as a tall, well-built young man with red-gold hair and grey eyes. He wears pale blue garments and golden chain mail, and carries a broad-bladed spear, a broadsword, and a sceptre. He is said to be able to summon many creatures to aid him in battle.

The faith appeals particularly to those who have suffered oppression, displacement, or the loss of their homes. Trithereon's following is growing rapidly in borderlands such as Highfolk, Sunndi, and northern Furyondy, and among the dispossessed peoples of Geoff, Sterich, the Ulek states, and the Shield Lands. Even in well-ordered lands such as the Yeomanry, his uncompromising defence of liberty attracts passionate converts. His cult is among the fastest-growing in the Flanaess.

#### Dogma

Every individual has the right to live freely and defend that freedom against those who would take it away. No ruler, nation, or institution has the right to impose tyranny upon others. Those who threaten the liberty or lives of others must be resisted, and wrongs must be answered rather than passively endured. Freedom requires vigilance, courage, and a willingness to fight when necessary. Those who have been dispossessed or oppressed must not surrender hope of reclaiming what was taken from them.

### The Priesthood

Trithereon's priests are active defenders of individual liberty, often serving as scouts, spies, skirmishers, and protectors of vulnerable communities. In rural and borderland regions, they work alongside woodsmen and demihumans to watch for despots, hostile humanoids, and other threats. In towns and cities, they teach self-protection and the use of weapons, practise battle tactics, and recruit experienced rangers and thieves to instruct them in covert conflict.

Services commonly involve ceremonial flames, the ringing of bells, displays of weaponry, and triumphant processions welcoming new converts. The faith favours action over elaborate theological discussion, and its priests are often found where liberty is threatened rather than safely established.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Trithereon is expected to:

- Defend individual liberty and resist tyranny, oppression, and unlawful imprisonment.
    
- Protect those unable to defend themselves against powerful aggressors.
    
- Remain vigilant against despots, hostile humanoids, and other threats to free communities.
    
- Train in the use of weapons and the practical defence of self and others.
    
- Help displaced people and dispossessed communities recover their homes and freedoms.
    
- Work with trustworthy allies, including woodsmen, demihumans, rangers, and other capable defenders.
    
- Hold oppressors accountable for their actions and pursue those who continue to threaten the lives or liberty of others.
    
- Avoid becoming an oppressor in the name of opposing oppression; the defence of freedom must not become a pretext for domination.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Trithereon frequently adventure to oppose tyrants, aid rebels and resistance movements, protect threatened settlements, and help displaced people reclaim their lands. They may undertake reconnaissance, sabotage, or armed action against oppressive powers. Others travel to teach communities how to defend themselves, or pursue those responsible for particularly grievous wrongs. Their commitment to liberty can bring them into conflict with lawful authorities, even when those authorities consider themselves just.

### Priest of Trithereon

**Ability Requirements:** Standard

**Prime Requisites:** Wisdom

**Alignment:** Chaotic good

**Weapons:** Any; spear (1st level), broadsword (2nd level)

**Armour:** Chain

**Major Spheres:** Astral, Charm, Combat, Guardian, Healing, Protection, Summoning

**Minor Spheres:** Chaos, Necromantic, Sun, Travellers

**Granted Powers**

**3rd Level — Backstab:** The priest can backstab with an edged weapon, inflicting double damage when the conditions for a backstab are met. The source does not specify whether this damage multiplier increases at higher levels.

**5th Level — Monster Summoning:** The priest may use _monster summoning_ spells from the wizard spell list as though they were priest spells of the same level. The precise spell levels available and their interaction with the reboot's magic system follow its general rules for spell access.

**8th Level — Animal Summoning:** The priest gains the ability to summon animals. The source gives no further details in this excerpt; the exact effect, available creatures, and frequency require the applicable power description or the reboot's general rules.

**Turn Undead:** Cannot turn undead.

## Ulaa

**Titles:** The Stone Woman

**Alignment:** LG

**Pantheon:** Flan

**Portfolio:** Earth, Mining, Quarries, Mountains, Miners

**Symbol:** A military pick crossed with a warhammer

Ulaa is the patron Power of miners, hillfolk, mountaineers, and quarrymen. She is also honoured by dwarves, gnomes, and others who labour beneath the earth or in the hills. She is commonly portrayed as a plain-faced, strong and determined woman with skin as hard as stone, clad in chain mail and carrying a military pick and great warhammer. Among demihumans she is often portrayed as a dwarf or gnome.

Ulaa's worship is strongest in hilly rural lands such as the Kron Hills and Flinty Hills. Her temples are commonly built underground, often within or alongside mines. Services include displays of gemstones and fine minerals, rhythmic hammering upon stone, and chanted hymns.

#### Dogma

The earth provides for those willing to work it, but its wealth must be won through labour, patience, and care. Stone is both foundation and obstacle: it supports homes and roads, shelters communities, and yields valuable resources, but can kill the careless. Ulaa's faithful are expected to respect the dangers of the earth while mastering the skills necessary to work within it.

Strength is not merely the ability to endure hardship, but the determination to protect others from it. Mines and quarries must be maintained, dangerous workings secured, and those who labour underground protected whenever possible.

### The Priesthood

Ulaa's priesthood is strongly community based. Her priests oversee mines and quarries, advise those responsible for their maintenance, and use their magic to protect workers from the dangers of the underground. They commonly cooperate with the priests of other peoples, particularly in communities where dwarves, gnomes, and humans work together.

Priests of Ulaa are expected to be practical and dependable. A mine that is unsafe, a neglected quarry, or workers sent into unnecessary danger is considered a failure of stewardship.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Ulaa is expected to:

- Help maintain safe mines, quarries, tunnels, and mountain roads.
    
- Protect miners, quarrymen, and other workers from supernatural and natural dangers.
    
- Encourage cooperation between communities and races that depend upon the earth's resources.
    
- Preserve useful knowledge concerning stone, minerals, mining, and underground hazards.
    
- Ensure that valuable resources are not wasted through carelessness or needless destruction.
    
- Aid communities whose livelihood depends upon mining and quarrying.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Priests of Ulaa adventure to protect mining communities, recover lost mineral wealth, investigate dangerous workings, and deal with creatures that threaten those who live and work in the hills. Many have extensive experience underground and are particularly well suited to expeditions involving mines, caves, and ruined subterranean settlements.

### Priest of Ulaa

**Ability Requirements:** Strength 13 or Constitution 14

**Alignment:** Lawful Good or Lawful Neutral

**Weapons:** Club, flail, hammer, military pick, sling, staff, warhammer

**Armour:** Any metal armour

**Major Spheres:** Combat, Divination, Elemental (earth, fire, water), Guardian, Healing, Protection, Wards

**Minor Spheres:** Necromantic

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Enemy of the Hills:** At 1st level, the priest must choose a racial enemy, normally a hill-dwelling humanoid or giant race such as ogres, gnolls, or orcs. The priest gains a bonus of +1 damage against creatures of the chosen race for every four levels of experience, rounding fractions up.

**1st Level — See the Hidden:** The priest may cast _detect invisible (objects)_ as a 2nd-level wizard.

**4th Level — Dig:** The priest may cast _dig_ as a 4th-level wizard.

**7th Level — Passwall:** The priest may cast _passwall_ as a 5th-level wizard.

**10th Level — Transmute Stone:** The priest may cast _transmute stone to flesh_ as a 6th-level wizard.

**12th Level — Stone's Endurance:** The priest becomes immune to attacks and effects belonging to the Elemental (earth) sphere.

**Turn Undead:** None

## Uvot

**Titles:** The Provider, The Bountiful Father, The Giver of Plenty

**Alignment:** Neutral Good

**Portfolio:** Prosperity, Bounty, Resources, Agriculture

**Symbol:** A sheaf of grain surrounded by fruits and tools

Uvot is the creator and leader of the Touv pantheon. He personifies the bounty of the land and the prosperity that can be achieved by understanding and properly using its gifts. He is the antithesis of needless destruction, teaching that the things provided by the world should be used wisely rather than wasted.

Uvot is normally depicted either as a Touv child surrounded by fruits, grains, and useful crafted objects, or as a great Touv man carrying these things in his arms.

#### Dogma

The world provides what people need, but its gifts must be understood and used wisely. Prosperity does not come from possessing the most, but from knowing how to make good use of what is available. Food should be grown, gathered, hunted, preserved, and shared. Materials should be crafted into useful objects, and knowledge should be passed from one generation to the next.

Nothing useful should be destroyed without reason. Uvot's followers oppose waste, needless destruction, and the deliberate ruin of resources that could sustain life. Even in hardship, there is usually something that can be salvaged, cultivated, traded, repaired, or put to another use.

Uvot's priests are forbidden to use magic that causes wanton destruction. Destructive magic may be used when necessary to protect life or secure the community, but destruction for amusement, spite, or convenience violates Uvot's teachings.

### The Priesthood

Uvot's priests and shamans are practical, resourceful people who use their knowledge to help their communities prosper. They teach medicine, advise farmers and hunters, help people locate water and useful plants, bless marriages, and show others how to make the best use of whatever resources are available.

A Uvot priest may know where the best soil lies, which plants can be eaten, where animals gather during the dry season, how to preserve food, or how to repair a broken tool. Their advice is not always requested, but it is almost always correct.

Uvot's priests also encourage cooperation and the responsible distribution of resources. Prosperity that leaves the community starving is not true prosperity.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Uvot is expected to:

- Help the community make wise and sustainable use of available resources.
    
- Teach agriculture, hunting, gathering, medicine, preservation, and useful crafts.
    
- Help people locate food, water, shelter, and other necessities.
    
- Discourage waste and the needless destruction of useful resources.
    
- Share useful knowledge with those who can benefit from it.
    
- Encourage productive cooperation and fair use of community resources.
    
- Bless marriages and support families and other stable communities.
    
- Preserve useful plants, animals, tools, and techniques whenever practical.
    
- Never use magic to cause wanton destruction.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Uvot's priests adventure in search of useful knowledge, rare resources, lost techniques, and places where their skills can help people survive and prosper. They may accompany explorers to identify useful plants and animals, find sources of water, establish new settlements, or recover tools and knowledge lost to disaster.

They are generally reluctant to destroy something merely because it is dangerous or inconvenient. A Uvot priest will first ask whether the threat can be contained, redirected, harvested, or otherwise put to useful purpose. Nevertheless, the preservation of resources never takes precedence over the lives of those who depend upon them.

### Priest of Uvot

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 14

**Alignment:** Neutral, Neutral Good, or Lawful Good

**Weapons:** Atlatl, club, dagger, hand axe, scythe, short bow, sickle, staff

**Armor:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Creation, Divination, Elemental, Guardian, Healing, Necromantic

**Minor Spheres:** Animal, Plant, Protection, Sun, Weather

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** A priest of Uvot must wear fine animal skins and cloth garments. These should be well made and maintained, but never ostentatious merely for the sake of display.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Knowledge of the Bounty:** The priest may use _locate animals or plants_ twice per day.

**3rd Level — Goodberry:** The priest may cast _goodberry_ once per day.

**5th Level — Plant Growth:** The priest may cast _plant growth_ once per day.

**7th Level — Blessed Abundance:** The priest may cast _blessed abundance_ once per day.

**10th Level — Commune with Nature:** The priest may cast _commune with nature_ once per day.

**13th Level — Heroes' Feast:** The priest may cast _heroes' feast_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** A priest of Uvot cannot turn undead.

## Vara

**Titles:** The Nightmare Queen, The Red-Eyed, Lady of Dread

**Alignment:** Neutral Evil

**Portfolio:** Nightmares, Fear, Oppression

**Symbol:** A red eye surrounded by black stars

Vara is the first child of the evil spirit Meyanok and considers herself superior to her younger siblings. She uses her birthright and supernatural power to force them to serve her purposes. Like her father, she hates the other great spirits of the Touv pantheon and delights in twisting their servants against them.

Vara is usually depicted as a Touv woman with red eyes and stars woven into her hair.

#### Dogma

Fear is one of the oldest and most reliable forms of power. Those who are afraid can be made to obey, and those who are terrified can be made to abandon everything they once believed. Vara teaches her followers to cultivate fear in others and to use it to establish dominance.

Cruelty is not merely permitted but useful. Humiliation breaks pride, nightmares destroy confidence, and uncertainty makes people dependent upon those who promise protection. Those who rule through fear must never allow their subjects to believe that resistance is safe.

Vara's followers should exploit weakness wherever they find it. Compassion toward the fearful is weakness unless it serves to make the victim more useful or more obedient.

### The Priesthood

The priests and shamans of Vara rule through fear and oppression. They serve tyrants, cruel chiefs, and other leaders who require enforcers capable of breaking resistance. Some establish their own small domains and terrorize those beneath them.

Many of Vara's willing followers are bullies who take pleasure in making others miserable. Others are simply cruel people who have discovered that Vara's teachings provide divine justification for their behavior.

Vara's priests cultivate an atmosphere of uncertainty and dread. A threat need not always be carried out; often the expectation of punishment is more effective. Dreams, rumors, frightening displays, and supernatural manifestations are all tools of the priesthood.

A priest of Vara may pray for illusion and phantasm spells normally restricted to the wizard's spell lists. Such spells are treated as **two levels higher** than their normal wizard level when determining the level at which the priest can cast them. Thus, _phantasmal force_, normally a 1st-level wizard spell, is treated as a 3rd-level spell for a priest of Vara.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Vara is expected to:

- Cultivate fear and uncertainty among those under his authority.
    
- Enforce the will of cruel rulers and leaders.
    
- Exploit the weaknesses and fears of others.
    
- Punish defiance harshly enough to discourage further resistance.
    
- Use nightmares, illusions, and supernatural terror to undermine opponents.
    
- Never allow subjects to become confident that resistance is safe.
    
- Reward obedience while making examples of those who defy the priesthood.
    
- Seek opportunities to undermine the servants of rival gods.
    
- Demonstrate Vara's superiority over those she considers weaker.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Vara's priests rarely adventure for altruistic reasons. They may accompany powerful patrons as agents and enforcers, seek magical objects capable of spreading fear, or infiltrate communities to undermine their leaders.

Some adventuring priests deliberately cultivate reputations for cruelty and supernatural terror. Others travel under false identities, using dreams and illusions to establish fear before revealing their allegiance. A Vara priest may also seek revenge against anyone who has humiliated or defied the priesthood.

### Priest of Vara

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 13

**Alignment:** Neutral Evil or Lawful Evil

**Weapons:** Atlatl, club, dagger, javelin, short sword

**Armor:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Charm, Combat, Elemental, Healing, Protection, Necromantic

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Divination, Elemental, Guardian, Necromantic, Plant, Sun, Weather

**Prohibited Spheres:**

**Magical Items:** A priest of Vara must wear a necklace made from mummified animal feet.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Instill Fear:** The priest may cast either _cause fear_ or _sleep_ once per day. _Cause fear_ is the reverse of _remove fear_.

**3rd Level — Scare:** The priest may cast _scare_ once per day.

**5th Level — Terror of the Mind:** The priest may cast either _cloak of fear_ or _phantasmal killer_ once per day. _Cloak of fear_ is the reverse of _cloak of bravery_.

**7th Level — Nightmare:** The priest may cast _nightmare_ once per day. This is the reverse of _dream_.

**10th Level — Eyebite:** The priest may cast _eyebite_ once per day.

**13th Level — Symbol of Despair:** The priest may cast either _symbol of hopelessness_ or _symbol of pain_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** A priest of Vara commands undead rather than turning them.

## Vatun

**Titles:** The North God, Lord of the North

**Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral

**Portfolio:** Northern barbarians, cold, winter, arctic beasts

**Symbol:** The Five Blades of Corusk

Vatun is the imprisoned god of the northern barbarians. For approximately seven hundred years he has been bound somewhere in the far north through the actions of priests of the Oeridian god Telchur. Little is known of his prison or of how aware Vatun remains of the world beyond it.

When he was free, Vatun was a whirlwind of cold rage and energy. He inspired the northern barbarian tribes to raid the warmer lands to the south and punished cowardly or disloyal tribes with bitter storms and monsters from the frozen wastes. Faithful tribes living near such settlements were left unharmed.

The northern barbarians preserve the legend of the **Five Blades of Corusk**, which, when united, are said to free Vatun from his imprisonment. According to the legend, the North God will then take vengeance upon Telchur and those responsible for his imprisonment, and lead his people southward in a great war against the warm lands.

Vatun appears as a huge man clad in polar bear furs, with a beard of ice and snow and frozen mist issuing from his mouth.

#### Dogma

The North makes no promises of comfort. Strength, courage, endurance, and loyalty are required of those who live beneath its cold skies. The weak must be hardened, the cowardly must be punished, and those who betray their people must expect the fury of winter.

Vatun's followers preserve the memory of their imprisoned god and await the day of his return. The northern peoples must remain strong until that time, for when Vatun is freed they will march behind him and reclaim what was taken from them.

### The Priesthood

Vatun's priests maintain his worship despite his imprisonment. They preserve the legends of the North God, especially the tale of the Five Blades of Corusk, and preach that Vatun will one day break his bonds and take vengeance upon those who betrayed him.

Because Vatun cannot presently grant his priests spells, their divine magic is severely restricted. Their faith alone sustains access to 1st- and 2nd-level priest spells. They receive none of the special abilities that emulate 3rd-level or higher spells while Vatun remains imprisoned.

Vatun's priests nevertheless continue to serve their people. They heal the injured, aid warriors in battle, endure the hardships of the northern wilderness, and preserve the traditions of the barbarian tribes. They may use any priest spell involving cold, regardless of its normal sphere, including _chill metal_.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Vatun is expected to:

- Preserve the legends and traditions of Vatun and the northern barbarians.
    
- Teach the story of Vatun's imprisonment and the Five Blades of Corusk.
    
- Prepare the northern peoples for Vatun's eventual return.
    
- Aid and heal members of their people.
    
- Stand beside their people in battle.
    
- Endure cold, hunger, hardship, and other trials without cowardice.
    
- Punish cowardice and betrayal when the priest has the authority to do so.
    
- Protect arctic beasts and other creatures associated with the northern wilderness.
    
- Seek information concerning Vatun's prison and the Five Blades of Corusk.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Vatun's priests adventure to preserve their people's traditions, search for the Five Blades of Corusk, investigate rumors concerning Vatun's prison, and oppose those who serve Telchur. Others travel south to learn about the peoples and lands that Vatun is destined to challenge upon his return.

Every such priest carries the knowledge that the god's imprisonment may not be permanent. Discovering a means to free Vatun is therefore among the greatest achievements a priest can accomplish.

### Priest of Vatun

**Ability Requirements:** CON 15

**Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral

**Weapons:** Any

**Armor:** Any armour permitted to northern barbarians

**Major Spheres:** All, Animal, Healing, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Divination, Protection, Sun, War

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Special Spell Access:** A priest of Vatun may cast any priest spell involving cold, regardless of its normal sphere. This includes _chill metal_.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Endure Cold:** The priest suffers no ill effects from ordinary cold and gains a +2 bonus to saving throws against magical cold.

**3rd Level — The Godless Winter:** Vatun is unable to grant his priests powers equivalent to 3rd-level or higher spells while he remains imprisoned. The abilities listed below are dormant until Vatun is freed.

**3rd Level — Resist the Cold:** If Vatun is freed, the priest may cast _resist cold_ or _fog cloud_ once per day. The fog cloud may instead manifest as the larger _fog bank_ variant.

**5th Level — Master of Beasts and Waters:** If Vatun is freed, the priest may cast _hold animal_ or _water walk_ once per day.

**7th Level — Form of the North:** If Vatun is freed, the priest may cast _wall of ice_ or _polymorph self_, with the latter limited to the form of a polar bear.

**10th Level — Wrath of Winter:** If Vatun is freed, the priest may cast _transmute rock to mud_ or _cone of cold_ once per day.

**13th Level — Call of the North:** If Vatun is freed, the priest may cast _conjure animals_ or _weather summoning_ once per day.

**Turn Undead:** Vatun's priests cannot turn or command undead.

**Holy Vestments:** Vatun's priests wear white furs or clothing trimmed with white fur. Their ceremonial dress emphasizes the harshness of the northern wilderness rather than elaborate decoration.

## Velnius

**Titles:** The Rainshroud, The Elder Breeze, Arch of the Sky, Sender of Rains

**Alignment:** N (NG tendencies)

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Sky, Weather, Rain

**Symbol:** A bird perched upon a cloud

Velnius is the eldest child of Procan and the leader of the Velaeri, the Oeridian deities responsible for the seasons, winds, and weather. He is commonly depicted as a tall, white-haired, middle-aged man wearing a feathered cloak from which rain and lightning pour. As the most responsible of his siblings, he frequently intervenes when their duties overwhelm them or they neglect their responsibilities.

Velnius governs the sky and all the weather beneath it. Rain nourishes parched fields, while sunshine dries flooded roads and sodden marshes. Storms and droughts bring hardship, but neither is inherently evil: all weather has its place in the world's cycles. His priests teach that the necessities of life flow from the dome of heaven, and that people must learn to live with the weather rather than demand that nature always accommodate them.

#### Dogma

The sky sustains the world. Rain, wind, cloud, lightning, and sunshine each serve their purpose, though their immediate effects may bring hardship. What appears destructive today may prove necessary tomorrow; what seems beneficial in one place may be disastrous in another.

Respect the weather and prepare for its changes. Help those whose livelihoods depend upon the sky, especially farmers, sailors, and travellers. Seek moderation when weather becomes extreme, and oppose those who deliberately corrupt natural weather for selfish ends. Do not curse the storm merely because it inconveniences you, nor praise the sunshine without considering those whose fields are dying of thirst.

The faithful should seek resolution through counsel before resorting to force. Yet patience is not submission: when provoked or when unnatural forces threaten the proper workings of the world, the Rainshroud's servants must act decisively.

### The Priesthood

Velnius's clergy are found throughout Oeridian lands, especially in agricultural communities and settlements dependent upon reliable weather. His worship is frequently combined with that of the other Velaeri, and many temples maintain shrines to all five deities. In coastal settlements, small shrines to Velnius are sometimes established near harbours, where sailors seek favourable winds and protection from storms.

His priests serve as weather-watchers, counsellors, mediators, and protectors of communities vulnerable to the elements. They advise farmers on seasonal preparations, pray for rain during droughts, and help communities prepare for storms and flooding. They may also travel considerable distances to investigate unnatural weather, confront those who manipulate the elements for destructive purposes, or restore conditions disrupted by magical interference.

Velnius's priests commonly speak on behalf of others, sometimes intervening even when their counsel has not been requested. They generally prefer negotiation to conflict, but those who mistake their patience for weakness may discover a formidable determination beneath it.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Velnius is expected to:

- Observe the weather and understand its effects upon the land and its inhabitants.
    
- Help communities prepare for droughts, storms, floods, and other dangerous conditions.
    
- Offer counsel to farmers, travellers, sailors, and others whose lives depend upon the weather.
    
- Encourage moderation and practical preparation rather than fear or resentment of natural weather.
    
- Seek peaceful resolutions to disputes and mediate when others cannot agree.
    
- Investigate and oppose unnatural weather or deliberate magical interference with natural conditions.
    
- Travel when necessary to aid communities threatened by unusual or destructive weather.
    
- Cooperate with the other Velaeri when the proper balance of the seasons is threatened.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring priests of Velnius travel to investigate abnormal weather, aid isolated settlements, and protect travellers from the elements. They may accompany agricultural expeditions, caravans, or coastal voyages, providing forecasts, practical advice, and magical assistance. Others seek the source of unnatural storms, pursue those who abuse weather-changing magic, or mediate disputes over water, farmland, and other resources affected by the climate.

A priest of Velnius may be patient and conciliatory in ordinary affairs, but becomes resolute when a community faces disaster or when someone deliberately turns the weather into a weapon.

### Priest of Velnius

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 12, Charisma 12

**Prime Requisites:** Wisdom

**Alignment:** Neutral, Neutral Good

**Weapons:** Shortspear (1st)

**Armour:** Any

**Required Proficiencies:** Weather Sense

**Bonus Proficiencies:** Agriculture, Direction Sense

**Recommended Proficiencies:** Animal Handling, Boating, Fishing, Navigation, Seamanship, Survival, Water Divination

**Major Spheres:** All, Elemental (Air, Water), Protection, Weather

**Minor Spheres:** Divination, Plant, Travellers

**Granted Powers**

**Weatherwise:** Rainshrouds have an exceptional sensitivity to changes in the weather. They receive a +2 bonus to Weather Sense proficiency checks and can recognize signs of an approaching storm or significant weather change when the relevant conditions are observable.

**3rd Level — Gust of Wind:** The Rainshroud can cast _gust of wind_ once per day.

**5th Level — Call Lightning:** The Rainshroud can cast _call lightning_ once per day, provided the surrounding weather and other conditions permit the spell to function normally.

**9th Level — Weather Prediction:** Once per day, the Rainshroud can determine the likely weather conditions in the surrounding region over the next 24 hours. This ability reveals the probable course of natural weather but does not automatically identify magical interference or guarantee an accurate prediction when supernatural forces are at work.

**Turn Undead:** As a priest of the same level.

  
  

## Vogan

**Titles:** The Storm Lord, the Roving Eye

**Alignment:** CG (N)

**Portfolio:** Weather, storms, rain, water

**Symbol:** A cascade of water

Vogan is the temperamental god of rain, storms, and water, and of the dependence of all living things upon moisture. He is worshipped to bring the rains that sustain life and to ward off the worst storms. He is depicted as a Touv man with laughing eyes and hair made of cascading water.

#### Dogma

Water is life, but water is never entirely tame. Rain may nourish crops and fill empty streams, or arrive as a destructive storm. Vogan's followers therefore respect both the necessity and the danger of water and weather. Clean water must be protected, befouled waters restored, and those who depend upon the same waters must learn to live together.

Vogan also embodies wandering, curiosity, and the unpredictable connections that arise between people. His priests are expected to keep their eyes open wherever they travel, observing the people and families they encounter and recognizing opportunities to bring them together.

### The Priesthood

Vogan's priests are guardians of clean water and practical intermediaries between communities. They clean befouled streams and ponds, seek out sources of clean water, and help communities make wise use of what water they possess. They also arrange marriages between families, clans, and tribes, using their travels and observations to identify promising matches.

Vogan's roving nature is reflected in his clergy. His priests are often travellers who carry news and observations from one community to another. They are as comfortable discussing the prospects of a marriage as the likelihood of tomorrow's rain.

Vogan grants his clerics special access to the forces of air and water. A Vogan priest may cast any priest spell involving air or water, regardless of the spell's normal sphere.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Vogan is expected to:

- Protect and restore sources of clean water, including streams, ponds, and springs.
    
- Seek out new sources of clean water when communities lack adequate supplies.
    
- Help communities prepare for dangerous storms while encouraging them to welcome necessary rain.
    
- Carry news and observations between communities during their travels.
    
- Encourage useful alliances and arrange suitable marriages between families, clans, and tribes.
    
- Never knowingly befoul a source of drinking water.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Vogan's priests frequently travel in search of new water sources, to carry warnings of approaching storms, or simply because the open road suits their nature. They may serve adventuring companions as scouts for weather and water, but their wandering also makes them useful intermediaries between otherwise distant communities. A Vogan priest may take particular interest in companions' personal relationships, especially when a promising match can be found among the people encountered during an adventure.

### Priest of Vogan

**Ability Requirements:** Constitution 14, Charisma 14

**Alignment:** CG, NG, N

**Weapons:** Atlatl, dagger, short bow, short sword, spear

**Armor:** Any nonmetal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Elemental (water, air), Healing

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Creation, Divination, Plant, Protection, Summoning, Sun, Weather

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Water Purification:** The priest may cast _purify food & drink_ three times per day, but the power affects water only. The priest may also cast _create water_ once per day.

**3rd Level — Obscurement:** The priest may cast _obscurement_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**5th Level — Breath or Passage:** The priest may choose either _water breathing_ or _water walk_ as a granted power. Once chosen, the selection cannot be changed.

**7th Level — Storm Protection:** The priest may choose either _protection from lightning_ or _raise water_ (the reverse of _lower water_) as a granted power.

**10th Level — Control Winds:** The priest may cast _control winds_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**13th Level — Weather Summoning:** The priest may cast _weather summoning_ as a priest of the appropriate level.

**Turn Undead:** Nil

## Wee Jas

**Witch Goddess, Ruby Sorceress, Stern Lady, Death's Guardian**

**Alignment:** Lawful Neutral

**Pantheon:** Suel

**Portfolio:** Magic, death, vanity, law

**Symbol:** A skull against a ruby background

Wee Jas is the Suel goddess of magic, death, vanity, and law. Where Boccob represents magic as a whole, Wee Jas is particularly concerned with the creation, preservation, and use of spells and magical items. She is always depicted as a strikingly beautiful Suel woman dressed in elaborate clothing and adorned with jewelry, often incorporating a skull motif.

Wee Jas is worshipped primarily by wizards, particularly those who study necromancy and magical lore. She is also revered by those who value strict order and by those who seek her protection for the dead. Wee Jas guards the departing soul rather than the corpse, and therefore does not object to the use of _animate dead_ or other magic that employs the remains of the deceased. She is even known to support those who seek lichdom, regarding the preservation of a skilled practitioner as a worthwhile application of magical power.

Wee Jas is stern and exacting. She expects her followers to respect established authority and obey those placed above them. Failure is more likely to bring punishment than success praise, and her punishments can be severe. She is allied with many lawful powers, including Boccob, Fortubo, Lendor, and Osprem, but has little patience for the chaotic gods. Her peculiar exception is Norebo, to whom she is strongly attracted despite his chaotic nature. Their supposed affair is a source of considerable gossip among the gods and their worshippers alike.

#### Dogma

Magic is the key to understanding yourself and the world. Through its study come knowledge, personal power, security, control over one's fate, and the ability to impose order upon chaos.

Respect those who came before you, for their knowledge and achievements made your own existence possible. Preserve what they have taught and add to it where you can. Death is not an enemy to be denied, but an inevitable transition that must be treated with dignity and respect. Those who possess power have obligations to use it responsibly, and those who hold authority must be obeyed unless that authority itself violates the proper order of things.

### The Priesthood

The followers of Wee Jas are collectively known as the **Jasadin**. Her clergy includes clerics, specialty priests, and wizards, with specialty priests forming the largest portion of the priestly hierarchy. Priests are commonly known as **magus** once they attain 9th level, while the senior priest of a temple is generally called the **high magus**.

Wee Jas's temples are uncommon outside the lands of the Scarlet Brotherhood, but within those lands a major city generally possesses a temple built in the manner of a wizard's tower. Temples contain extensive libraries of magical lore and records of local law, and are decorated with elegant statuary, discreet skull motifs, gemstones, and magical fires.

The priesthood serves as arbitrators, magical advisers, researchers, and keepers of the dead. Priests conduct funerals, investigate magical phenomena, maintain wards and magical defenses, and preserve records of law and magical knowledge.

All clerics and specialty priests of Wee Jas gain **Religion (Suel)** as a bonus nonweapon proficiency. Non-priest clergy who serve the faith may cast a cantrip once per day.

Wee Jas requires her priests to seek her permission before commanding undead or attempting to raise or resurrect the dead. A priest must use _commune_ to seek her judgment. She rarely permits the resurrection of a non-lawful character and is especially reluctant to aid a chaotic one. She is likewise selective about the use of commanded undead, generally permitting it when the undead are lawful or mindless and their service advances the interests of law, magic, or death. A priest who deliberately ignores her judgment loses one experience level and three levels of spellcasting ability until an appropriate _atonement_ is completed.

Followers of lower rank are expected to obey the legitimate orders of their superiors.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Wee Jas is expected to:

- study and preserve magical knowledge;
    
- respect the lawful authority of the church and those properly placed above them;
    
- arbitrate disputes and uphold legitimate laws;
    
- administer funerals and safeguard the dignity of the dead;
    
- investigate magical phenomena and unusual applications of magic;
    
- preserve records, lore, and traditions for those who will follow;
    
- maintain the magical defenses of temples and communities when capable;
    
- use magic deliberately and responsibly rather than treating it as a toy or novelty;
    
- seek permission before commanding undead or interfering with the normal passage of the dead.
    

Waxing moons are holy nights, representing the beauty of the heavens. On nights when both moons are waxing, particularly elaborate magical fires and illusions are used in her honor. Coldeven 4th, known as the **Goddess's Blush**, is the most important of these observances; the most valuable piece of jewelry discovered by the faithful during the preceding year is traditionally offered to Wee Jas in a sacred fire.

#### Adventuring Priests

Adventuring priests commonly seek lost magical knowledge, investigate unusual magical phenomena, recover ancient artifacts, settle disputes involving magic or inheritance, or protect places and people important to the faith. Some travel to study unfamiliar magical traditions, while others seek dangerous magical objects that should be secured rather than destroyed.

Priests who specialize in death magic may undertake expeditions to recover necromantic lore, protect tombs, or investigate disturbances involving the dead. Such priests distinguish between protecting the souls of the departed and preserving their physical remains.

### Priest of Wee Jas

**Ability Requirements:** Intelligence 13, Wisdom 9

**Alignment:** Lawful Neutral or Lawful Evil

**Weapons:** As wizards

**Armor:** None

**Major Spheres:** All, Astral, Charm, Divination, Elemental (All), Guardian, Healing, Law, Necromantic, Protection, Summoning, Thought, Time

**Minor Spheres:** Combat, Sun, Wards

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Magical Items:** As clerics; beginning at 9th level, Karuth may also use magical items normally restricted to wizards.

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Alteration of the Self:** Once per day, the priest may alter one ability score of a creature by 1 point for 1 round per level. The ability score may be raised or lowered, but cannot be reduced below 3 or increased above 18. An unwilling target may attempt a saving throw vs. spell to resist.

**3rd Level — Spell Resistance:** The priest gains a +1 bonus to all saving throws against spells.

**6th Level — Lesser Arcane Study:** The priest may pray for 1st- and 2nd-level wizard spells from the Enchantment/Charm or Illusion/Phantasm schools as though they were priest spells of the same level.

**9th Level — Greater Arcane Study:** The priest may pray for 1st- through 4th-level wizard spells from the Alteration, Enchantment/Charm, Illusion, and Invocation/Evocation schools as though they were priest spells of the same level. The priest may also use magical items normally restricted to wizards.

**13th Level — Magic Resistance:** The priest gains 5% magic resistance. This increases by 1% for every level gained thereafter.

**Optional Devotion — Swift Casting:** A priest who accepts a 10% experience penalty may reduce the casting time of priest spells whose casting time is less than one round. This benefit does not apply to wizard spells gained through Arcane Study. If the priest abandons the experience penalty, this ability is lost until the penalty has again been maintained for one full year.

The reduction is determined by level:

- **1st–5th level:** Reduce casting time of 1st- and 2nd-level spells by 1.
    
- **6th–10th level:** Reduce casting time of 1st- and 2nd-level spells by 2.
    
- **11th–15th level:** Reduce casting time of 1st- and 2nd-lev-el spells by 2; reduce 3rd- and 4th-level spells by 1.
    
- **16th level and higher:** Reduce casting time of 1st- through 4th-level spells by 2; reduce 5th-level spells by 1.
    

Casting time cannot be reduced below 1.

### Holy Vestments

Priests of Wee Jas traditionally wear layered, full-length robes alternating between gray and black. Hoods are customary but normally worn back from the face. Jewelry is prominent, particularly gemstones and ornaments bearing skulls, and priests commonly carry ornate staves.

Adventuring priests generally wear gray or black robes, decorated with runes, jewelry, or other symbols appropriate to their station.

## Wenta

**Titles:** The Alewife, Harvest Daughter

**Alignment:** Chaotic Good

**Pantheon:** Oeridian

**Portfolio:** Autumn, West Wind, Harvest, Brewing

**Symbol:** A large mug of beer

Wenta is the youngest of the Velaeri, the Oeridian gods of wind and agriculture, and a daughter of Procan. She appears as a rosy-cheeked, buxom young woman with straw in her hair and a large mug of beer in hand. Free-spirited and sociable, she follows her heart rather than settling into lasting attachments. Of her siblings, she dislikes Telchur most, regarding him as a persistent shadow whose winter follows too closely upon her autumn.

#### Dogma

The cool winds of autumn announce that it is time to reap. Work hard while the harvest is ready, share the labour fairly, and ensure that the work is finished in time for everyone to enjoy its rewards. Brewing deserves as much care and devotion as a lover, and good ale can warm the body and lift the spirits against the coming winter. Hard work should be followed by pleasant evenings, good friends, and plenty of good drink.

### The Priesthood

Wenta’s clergy are friendly, outgoing, and unafraid to speak with strangers. They are found among farmers, harvesters, brewers, vintners, innkeepers, and those who simply appreciate a good gathering. Many serve as brewers or brewmasters, while others travel to sample local drinks, visit old friends, and make new ones.

Priests organize communal celebrations, oversee harvest work to ensure that labour is shared fairly, and encourage people to set aside their burdens once the work is done. Their worship is often informal, with feasting, music, conversation, and drinking taking precedence over elaborate ceremony. They pray for their spells during a brief interval before sundown. The week of Brewfest is especially sacred, though Wenta would rather her followers celebrate than spend the whole occasion in solemn worship. The priesthood’s blessed water takes the form of blessed ale or beer. The club is their favoured weapon.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Wenta is expected to:

- Help bring in the harvest and see that its work is shared fairly.
    
- Encourage the proper care and craft of brewing, and respect the labour behind food and drink.
    
- Bring people together in fellowship, particularly after a day’s work.
    
- Welcome strangers and cultivate friendships beyond their own communities.
    
- Celebrate the turning of the seasons and help others face the coming winter with good spirits.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Wenta’s priests adventure for many reasons: to visit friends, discover unfamiliar lands and their local brews, help a community through a difficult harvest, or simply pursue an opportunity that sounded excellent at the time. Their sociability makes them natural hosts, negotiators, and companions, though their enthusiasm for drink can sometimes lead them into ill-considered promises or unexpected trouble.

### Priest of Wenta

_The following mechanics are a proposed Reboot interpretation; a complete AD&D 2e specialty-priest stat block has not been verified._

**Ability Requirements:** Constitution 12, Charisma 12

**Prime Requisites:** Wisdom, Charisma

**Alignment:** Chaotic Good

**Weapons:** Club (favoured); sickle, staff, and other simple weapons

**Armour:** Any

**Major Spheres:** All, Air, Plant

**Minor Spheres:** Chaos, Charm, Good, Healing

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Brewer’s Calling:** The priest gains a +2 bonus to proficiency checks involving Brewing. If the reboot’s proficiency rules distinguish quality, the priest may also identify obvious flaws in a batch of beer or ale by tasting it.

**3rd Level — Harvest Fellowship:** Once per day, the priest may use _friends_ as a priest spell. The effect represents Wenta’s gift for warmth and conviviality, not supernatural compulsion to drink or celebrate.

**5th Level — Favourable Breeze:** Once per day, the priest may call upon a mild, favourable wind for up to one turn per level. It can cool a hot worksite, fill sails, disperse smoke, or aid the winnowing of grain, but cannot produce a dangerous gust or directly damage creatures.

**7th Level — Blessed Brew:** The priest may bless a quantity of ale or beer equivalent to the normal amount of holy water created by a priest of the same level. The brew functions as holy water for purposes appropriate to its use; it is not ordinary drink once blessed.

**Turn Undead:** As a priest of the same level.

## Xan Yae

**Lady of Perfection, Supreme Mistress of Petals**

**Alignment:** Neutral

**Portfolio:** Twilight, shadows, stealth, discipline, harmony, mental power

**Symbol:** A black lotus blossom

Xan Yae is the Baklunish goddess of twilight, shadows, stealth, discipline, and the power of the mind. She teaches that the mind possesses greater potential than most mortals understand, and that through discipline it can achieve harmony with the self, the world, and ultimately the greater pattern of existence.

Xan Yae is associated with the transition between day and night. Twilight represents the point at which neither light nor darkness has completely overcome the other, and is therefore sacred to her followers. It is a time of uncertainty and mystery, but also of balance and quiet. Her followers learn to move through this uncertainty without fear, relying upon discipline rather than force or impulse.

The faith of Xan Yae rests upon three related principles: **the Universal Mind, Perpetual Harmony, and Internal Peace**. The Universal Mind teaches that consciousness and imagination are fundamental to existence, and that disciplined minds possess the potential to influence reality. Perpetual Harmony teaches that all things exist within relationships of balance and that a person who understands themselves and their surroundings can act without unnecessary conflict. Internal Peace is the foundation of both: only a mind free from distraction, fear, and uncontrolled desire can achieve either harmony or mastery.

These teachings have made Xan Yae particularly popular among monks, martial artists, mystics, philosophers, psionicists, and others who seek mastery of body and mind. Thieves and other practitioners of stealth sometimes invoke her as well, although her priests distinguish disciplined concealment from simple criminality.

Xan Yae has little interest in the disputes of the gods unless they threaten the stability of the world. She opposes both Pyremius and Pholtus because each represents an extreme that, in her view, threatens the harmony of existence: destructive darkness in the first case and uncompromising light in the second. She therefore works quietly to restrain forces that threaten to overwhelm their opposites.

Xan Yae is traditionally depicted as a beautiful Baklunish woman dressed in flowing garments, although she may also appear as an acrobat, martial artist, or other seemingly ordinary traveler. She is rarely seen without her matched falchions, the **Butterfly Blades**, although she normally keeps them concealed.

#### Dogma

Master yourself before attempting to master anything else. The uncontrolled mind is a source of weakness; the disciplined mind is capable of extraordinary things.

Seek **Internal Peace**. Control fear, anger, desire, pride, and distraction rather than allowing them to control you. Physical training and mental discipline are different expressions of the same pursuit.

Seek **Perpetual Harmony**. Understand the forces around you before acting against them. Do not waste strength where precision will suffice, and do not create conflict merely to demonstrate your power.

Walk between extremes. Light and darkness, action and stillness, strength and subtlety, body and mind all have their proper places. Neither should be allowed to consume its opposite.

Use stealth as a discipline rather than an indulgence. The unseen hand can accomplish what brute force cannot, but secrecy, like every other tool, should serve a purpose.

The disciplined mind can change the world. First change yourself; then learn what the world can become.

### The Priesthood

The priesthood of Xan Yae is small and decentralized. Many priests live in monasteries or secluded communities on the fringes of settled Baklunish lands, although individual priests may travel widely in search of teachers, students, forgotten knowledge, or opportunities to restore harmony where it has been disturbed.

Priests divide their training between physical and mental disciplines. Meditation, controlled breathing, martial exercises, balance, acrobatics, and weapons practice are treated as complementary rather than separate pursuits. A priest who possesses great physical strength but cannot control their emotions is considered no more accomplished than a scholar whose intellect is undisciplined.

The priesthood has no universal requirement that its members be pacifists. Violence is acceptable when necessary, but wasteful violence is regarded as evidence of poor discipline. A precise action that resolves a conflict is preferable to a prolonged struggle, regardless of whether the action is accomplished through diplomacy, stealth, martial skill, or magic.

Priests of Xan Yae frequently serve as teachers, wandering philosophers, martial instructors, scouts, messengers, and mediators. Some deliberately seek out conflicts between opposing factions in order to prevent either side from becoming dominant.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Xan Yae is expected to:

- cultivate mastery of both body and mind;
    
- practice meditation and self-discipline regularly;
    
- maintain control over fear, anger, pride, and other disruptive emotions;
    
- seek harmony within themselves and their surroundings;
    
- avoid unnecessary displays of power;
    
- use stealth and deception only with deliberate purpose;
    
- intervene when an extreme threatens to destroy the balance of a community or region;
    
- teach useful knowledge and disciplined practice to those willing to learn;
    
- seek understanding before resorting to force;
    
- preserve the teachings of the Way of the Lotus;
    
- continually test their own limitations rather than assuming that mastery has been achieved.
    

A priest is not expected to remain neutral when confronted with cruelty, tyranny, or destructive evil. Xan Yae's teachings concern **harmony**, not moral indifference. A force that threatens to destroy the balance of a community may properly be opposed, but the priest should seek the least wasteful and most controlled means of doing so.

#### Adventuring Priests

Xan Yae's priests frequently travel in search of knowledge, worthy teachers, forgotten monasteries, lost martial traditions, or situations in which their skills can be tested. Some deliberately seek difficult journeys because hardship exposes weaknesses that cannot be discovered through meditation alone.

Others become involved in conflicts between powerful factions. Such priests may attempt to prevent either side from becoming dominant, particularly when victory by one faction would threaten the stability of an entire region.

A priest may also adventure simply to teach. Traveling through isolated settlements, they may establish schools, teach martial arts, instruct students in meditation, or introduce disciplined methods of study to communities that have little access to formal education.

### Priest of Xan Yae

**Ability Requirements:** Wisdom 14

**Alignment:** Neutral or Lawful Neutral

**Weapons:** Falchion, quarterstaff, lasso, dart, sai, chain

**Armor:** None

**Required Proficiencies:** Falchion

**Major Spheres:** All, Astral, Charm, Divination, Law, Numbers, Thought, Time

**Minor Spheres:** Healing, Protection

**Prohibited Spheres:** None

**Granted Powers**

**1st Level — Silent Step:** The priest may move silently as a thief of the same level.

**1st Level — Rogue Training:** The priest may purchase Rogue proficiencies without paying an additional proficiency cost.

**3rd Level — Martial Discipline:** The priest may acquire continuing specialization in an unarmed fighting style as though a warrior of the same level. This represents the priest's continued martial training rather than a change of class.

**5th Level — Guarded Mind:** The priest gains a +1 bonus to saving throws against ESP, thought-reading, and other magical effects that directly attempt to read or control the priest's thoughts.

**7th Level — Disciplined Body:** The priest becomes immune to nonmagical disease.

**9th Level — Shadow Door:** The priest may cast _shadow door_ once per day as a granted power.

**14th Level — Shadowcat:** The priest may cast _shadowcat_ once per day as a granted power.

### Holy Days and Ceremonies

**Twilight** is the sacred time of Xan Yae. Daily prayers and most formal religious ceremonies are performed at dusk, when day gives way to night without either immediately overcoming the other.

The **Autumn Equinox** is the principal holy day of the faith. It marks the equal division of daylight and darkness. Ceremonies are held at both dawn and dusk, and new initiates are formally admitted into the greater religious community during these observances.

The **27th of Harvester**, known as the **Day of the Legion Will**, is a more unusual observance. Followers spend the day in meditation, attempting to unite their individual concentration into a shared act of mental discipline. Monasteries may maintain silence from sunrise to sunset, with participants seeking a state of collective awareness that they believe brings them closer to the Universal Mind.

### The Way of the Lotus

Among the Baklunish peoples, particularly in the more remote regions and the Distant West, Xan Yae's teachings have produced a distinct martial tradition known as the **Way of the Lotus**. The tradition treats physical combat as another means of cultivating Internal Peace and Perpetual Harmony.

Practitioners emphasize economy of movement, balance, precision, and control rather than displays of brute strength. The tradition is practiced by monks and martial artists, but its principles have also been adopted by warriors, nobles, and ordinary villagers.

For a follower of Xan Yae, mastery is demonstrated not by how much force one can exert, but by how little force is required to accomplish what must be done.

## Xanag

**Titles:** The Golden Lady

**Alignment:** N

**Portfolio:** Metals, beauty, craftsmanship

**Symbol:** A golden woman surrounded by radiant light

Xanag represents the bounty of the earth transformed by fire: metal, and the beauty of things fashioned from it. She is an indifferent goddess, easily distracted by superficialities and the outward beauty of people and things. She is always depicted as a Touv woman seemingly made entirely of gold, surrounded by radiant light.

#### Dogma

The earth provides the raw materials from which beauty and usefulness may be created. Metal must therefore be respected, mastered, and transformed into objects worthy of admiration. Xanag's followers value skill, refinement, ornamentation, and craftsmanship, believing that even practical objects can be made beautiful.

Yet Xanag is not a goddess of profound wisdom or self-denial. She is easily captivated by appearances, and her priests often share her appreciation for fine clothing, beautiful objects, precious metals, and impressive craftsmanship.

### The Priesthood

Xanag's priests are commonly metalsmiths, jewellers, artisans, or connoisseurs of fine objects. They teach the secrets of metalsmithing and jewellery-making and encourage skilled craftsmanship among the Touv. Poets and lovers also pray to Xanag, seeking beauty in both art and affection.

Her priests frequently officiate marriages, particularly when the union is celebrated with fine ornaments or other works of craftsmanship. A Xanag priest may regard the beauty of a wedding ceremony as nearly as important as the ceremony's religious significance.

#### Duties and Obligations

A priest of Xanag is expected to:

- Learn and preserve the secrets of metalsmithing and jewellery-making.
    
- Encourage skilled craftsmanship and appreciation of beautiful things.
    
- Maintain and improve works of exceptional beauty or craftsmanship.
    
- Officiate marriages when called upon.
    
- Encourage poets, artists, artisans, and lovers in their pursuits.
    
- Treat fine workmanship and precious materials with respect.
    

#### Adventuring Priests

Xanag's priests may adventure to obtain rare metals, gemstones, or unusual materials for their craft. Others travel to study the finest works produced by distant peoples or to seek beautiful objects worthy of their goddess. Some accompany adventurers simply because they are attracted by the prospect of discovering something rare, valuable, or beautiful.

### Priest of Xanag

**Ability Requirements:** Charisma 15

**Alignment:** N, LN

**Weapons:** Atlatl, dagger, short sword, spear

**Armor:** Any metal armour

**Major Spheres:** All, Charm, Combat, Divination, Healing

**Minor Spheres:** Elemental (earth, fire), Protection, Sun
